- candidate chunk-35faa3dc54bb97cc3c2bc9f644db1de3 doc=doc-2dca4aebd2d58cec651996f808e3aec7 sections=[Tier > Tier > Mods by Tier] text="LV.\n- ↑ Gendustry requires significant bee breeding advancements (plutonium, uranium, naquadah, etc.) to progress and is mostly gated to LuV+. - candidate chunk-8f1d9e82bcbbde086d1be19a8f46f7c0 doc=doc-8666c624815c422fd88b0cdfb5cbfb2a sections=[High Voltage (HV) > Side Objectives > Blood Magic] text="Blood Magic\nIf you are doing a no-rocket run, this mod is one of the only avenues to progress into EV, as summoning meteors is how you will obtain resources such as Titanium. Doing so may increase your warp effects."
- candidate chunk-48821e45dbe567c3c4946b397bd05ac8 doc=doc-626c925b618145e4e43655f7673b36b4 sections=[Low Voltage (LV) > Progression] text="Progression\nProgression to MV requires the player to obtain 3 essential materials:\n• Gallium -\nThere is no renewable way to obtain Gallium in LV. The best way is to electrolyze Sphalerite dust, giving an 11% chance for Gallium. Gallium is also obtainable as a byproduct of Zinc ore processing, Zinc Gravel can be purchased with Blacksmith coins at regular enough intervals that it shouldn't be a major issue for now. You can also use XP buckets if you're desperate. With an HV macerator you can obtain more byproducts from ore processing, making Gallium much easier to obtain.\n• Arsenic -\nCobaltite is the primary way of obtaining Arsenic in LV. When Cobaltite is blasted in an EBF with oxygen it produces roasted cobalt and arsenic dust. Running roasted arsenic dust through the EBF again results in 3 arsenic ingots per 2 cobaltite. This process is oxygen neutral after electrolyzing the carbon dioxide produced.\n• Aluminum -\nAluminum has 2 ways to be obtained in LV, one involves compressing Aluminum dust and blasting the \"raw Aluminum\" into 1 Aluminum ingot. This recipe is slow and not recommended. The other recipe uses 10 Alumina and 5 cryolite to produce 4 Aluminum ingots. While being more involved, this recipe offers much faster and more power efficient Aluminum."
- candidate chunk-f9a7893bcc78ce6dce97919745883b55 doc=doc-8666c624815c422fd88b0cdfb5cbfb2a sections=[High Voltage (HV) > Progression > End Of HV] text="End Of HV\nAfter collection of ores from the Moon, check NEI on how to make Titanium (you process everything into Rutile then react it with Carbon Dust and Chlorine). You will need a stack for turning in the titanium quest to enter the EV tier in the questbook. Note that you must cool Titanium using a Vacuum Freezer after smelting in the EBF.\nSpending some time now looking for a Moon dungeon is an excellent idea too. The mobs are not tough if you've been keeping up at all with your armor, weapons, and health bar. In addition, you are guaranteed to find a T2 rocket schematic in the loot chest (which you absolutely need to progress out of early EV in a standard run), and the quest book will grant you access to your own personal dimension!\nInfrastructure for processing Rutile into Titanium, and Bauxite specifically is recommended, as they are relatively simple multi-step processes and their reactants (not all of Bauxite's, though) can be recycled very simply. Remember, you will be consuming dozens of stacks of Titanium in EV!"
- candidate chunk-a5f28cb4868a57c9395131f83cfe3cd6 doc=doc-d38e0fb2c49edead8e172a0081f57d61 sections=[Extreme Voltage (EV) > Progression > Your second rocket / Late-EV] text="Your second rocket / Late-EV\nAfter getting the EV field generator you needed for the rocket computer, it's time to build your second rocket. It's a pretty straightforward step. If you forgot or didn't get much meteoric iron, you have to get back to the moon and mine some. I recommend you to have 12 stacks or 45 stacks if you don't want to go back to the moon again for meteoric iron (also for t3 rocket). With the exception of several new parts, which are booster and t2 landing module, the rest goes the same as t1 rocket but is more expensive.\nYou only need a trip to mars for you to get all the resources needed. Also, T2 lander doesn't need you to slow down when approaching as it's a balloon that bounces after it reaches the surface. Your mission there is to collect desh vein (it also has tungsten ore), tungsten vein (also provides lithium), and pitchblende vein (also provides thorium) if you didn't get enough uranium by centrifuging thorium ore. Remember to explore mars more for finding the mars dungeon which contains the T3 rocket blueprint."
- candidate chunk-5ff54a6f190364d05242ad8bcac58ed8 doc=doc-0b20024c57c212406bf68b660f2eaf30 sections=[Rockets > Crafting Tier 2+ Rockets] text="Crafting Tier 2+ Rockets\nEntrance to a Moon Dungeon\nThe NASA Workbench requires new schematics to craft higher tiers of rockets. These schematics are found in dungeons on planets in tier order. Tier 2 rocket schematics, for example, can be found in the reward room of Moon Dungeons. Dungeon entrances are deep holes on the surface that have unnatural brick blocks on the bottom.\nThe dungeons are naturally generated and so may be difficult to find. It is important to mark the location of each dungeon found as the coordinates are the same across each planet.\nHigher tiers of rockets require different kinds of Rocket Fuels ."
- candidate chunk-8020be5e3374a08380c78b2c3d21be17 doc=doc-626c925b618145e4e43655f7673b36b4 sections=[Low Voltage (LV)] text="Low Voltage (LV) is the first electric tier in GregTech: New Horizons. With access to electricity comes new hazards and mechanics, but also far more variety of specialized machines that can process materials into new parts with greater efficiency."
- candidate chunk-c417ab6d787b9adf11daa32bbf57bcee doc=doc-0acf46d9067c834f0c19087002ed00b0 sections=[Insane Voltage (IV) > Progression Goals > Magic Mods] text="Magic Mods\nIV is also the point at which much of the lategame content in many magic mods becomes available to players willing to invest into them.\nIn Thaumcraft , most of the Kami equipment that requires Ichor becomes available with Osmiridium, an alloy that is made from Osmium and Iridium and smelted in an LuV-tier EBF. This includes the best available wand materials, a set of powerful tools and armor, and the Pure Tear and Purification Talisman, both of which can be used to solve warp (the former once and the latter continuously). The Pure Tear can also be obtained through the Quest Book using one Osmiridium Ingot, which can help players struggling with warp who aren't close to obtaining Ichor and being able to craft it themselves.\nIn Blood Magic , the tier 5 and 6 Blood Orbs can be crafted once platline materials are obtained, which unlocks the ability to summon many more meteors and to reach almost all of the mod's content, including advanced rituals and spell parts. Furthermore, some of the most useful meteors require IV machines or components to summon, such as the IV Scanner meteor, which contains huge amounts of platline dusts.\nIn Botania , IV is the first tier where it's possible to summon the Gaia Guardian, although the naquadah that is required can make it challenging to afford until a later tier."
- candidate chunk-ecf1c03449abec6843b9508086ea1f0d doc=doc-709fa05c9dc5e6bdd1e4979266413ad2 sections=[Power Generation > Solar Panels] text="Solar Panels\nSolar panels are available in tiers from 1 EU/t all the way up to UV (524288 EU/t). Creating a solar panel of a given tier requires you to be 2 tiers higher than the panel (i.e. to make a LV panel you need to be HV). Solars are a viable power source from MV up to UV, but are never a main power source due to the tier requirement, however they can nicely help your regular power gen, especially in operations that need to be running all the time or far away from your main power source.\nProduction: Solars up to ULV are crafted in the regular crafting grid, then in the Solar Factory multiblock. Panels are quite expensive to make up for not needing fuel, especially more so if you're making them without the previous solar panel.\nUsage: Solar panels can be placed as a cover on a GT machine, battery buffer, machine hull or even cable, or crafted into a standalone solar panel block. Cover-type solars need periodic cleaning by right-clicking with empty hand while having a bucket of water in your inventory (can be done by golems), or their output will decrease. Solar panel blocks lack this disadvantage, also you don't need to protect them from rain.\nThaumcraft offers an option to make compressed solar panels. These take 8 panels of the previous tier to produce 10 times more power, so even if recipes become exponentially more expensive there's a bit of advantage. Octuple compressed solar panels can produce 100,000,000 EU/t, which is almost 200 times more than a UV solar panel. Moreover, there are infused versions of compressed solars which produce even more power under certain conditions. For example, octuple aqua infused solar can produce up to 600,000,000 EU/t while in thunderstorm. These also have the added bonus of being very TPS efficient, which is very important late game.\nStarting from triple onwards, it will require infusion. Read the Infusion automation guide for help on automating these solars. The maximum tier is 8 (Octuple), and requires 16777216 1 volt solars. This spreadsheet lists all items needed to create an UV Solar Panel and an Octuple Compressed Solar. The sheet is outdated for the former, since it was made before solar factory was added."
- candidate chunk-c4f515f28759c9259bd668bf7140e214 doc=doc-a9f4f7813df646e0e5f0ab08ab29b50e sections=[Ludicrous Voltage (LuV)] text="Ludicrous Voltage (LuV) is the sixth electric tier in GregTech: New Horizons. This tier is largely centered around building the infrastructure necessary to construct the first tier of Fusion Reactor , as well as the new items and machines gated by fusion. This is the first tier where the Assembly Line is used to craft on-tier components as well as highly advanced machines. In addition, the costs and crafting trees for machines are even larger than in previous tiers, making fully automated on-demand crafting with AE2 absolutely necessary. The massive demand for robust infrastructure continues for the rest of the game; arrays of Volcanus consuming on-tier voltage are highly suggested."

- candidate chunk-e53a7f7cd08cccca5de892a38320bc77 doc=doc-8666c624815c422fd88b0cdfb5cbfb2a sections=[High Voltage (HV)] text="High Voltage (HV) is the third electric tier in GregTech: New Horizons. In this tier, several new mechanics are introduced to you such as cleanrooms , space travel , and multiblock versions of your singleblock machines. With the introduction of the Cleanroom, your ability to produce more complex circuits and some of their critical components will be restricted to the inside of the Cleanroom multiblock structure, or other multiblocks that are also considered cleanrooms. Many important materials, such as titanium, can only be extracted from ores that exist on other planets and celestial bodies. You will find that all of these mechanics will carry on with you for the foreseeable future.\nUpon reaching HV, you should either have the ability to produce large quantities of stainless steel or quickly plan on developing the infrastructure to do so, as this is the first tier that starts demanding large quantities of its respective machine hull material, whether it's through the numerous single block machines or new multi-block machines such as the expensive but rewarding Distillation Tower."
- candidate chunk-47d289c4d1a14c32f00981954a28a1d8 doc=doc-10abece1affc7c7ad6fb4e0605bf710b sections=[Ore Processing Concepts > How To Do "Good" Ore Processing? > Flexible] text="Flexible\nOres in GT: New Horizons have multiple \" routes \" you can take which give different outputs. (See the bauxite chart to the right.) For bauxite you can take three routes (Note that these choices simply outline the basic bauxite processing and are not meant to show the \"best\" way to process bauxite, realistically you will probably want to do chembath and/or bauxite slurry):\n- Macerate -> Macerate -> Simple Wash/Centrifuge (Grossular byproduct)\n- Macerate -> Thermal Centrifuge -> Macerate (Rutile + Gallium byproduct)\n- Macerate -> Simple/Normal Wash -> Macerate -> Simple Wash/Centrifuge (Rutile + Grossular (if normal wash) byproduct)\nWhile for most bases, Rutile + Gallium Route 2 is superior, perhaps you already have automated gallium from essence berries. Then you might decide to do Route 3 instead, as it gives 2x more Rutile and therefore 2x more Titanium.\nEvery ore in the game will have a different \"optimal\" route for your run and base needs. This may also change over time. As a result, you want ore processing systems to be flexible - allowing different ores to go on different routes.\nYou also want to be able to automatically pull out things at any step of the process. For example, Rare Earths from GT++ require much higher power load, which would cause your existing macerator line to choke without modifications. You'll want to filter these out and process them in specially dedicated macerators."

- candidate chunk-137405157af87dd0f3eee24e6971f51d doc=doc-626c925b618145e4e43655f7673b36b4 sections=[Low Voltage (LV) > GT Electric Machines] text="GT Electric Machines\nMachine GUI. Auto-output buttons circled in yellow.\nLV Electric machines have some new features that were not available with Steam-age machinery. For example, the steam exhaust is replaced with an automatic output. This output's facing can be changed with a wrench similar to how the exhaust was configured on Steam machines. Just like their Steam counterparts, all electric GT machines cannot be input or output from their front face. To enable automatic output, open the GUI of the machine and click on the button at the lower left corner. There are two automatic output toggle buttons; the left one is for liquid and the right one is for items.\nElectrolyzer clay dust recipe. The MV power requirement is circled in yellow.\nGregTech machinery is divided by tier, which corresponds to a specific voltage - LV (32 V), MV (128 V), HV (512 V) etc. Most recipes are gated to a specific voltage/tier or higher, which is shown with the recipe in NEI (Not Enough Items). If the recipe requires more than one amp it will also be listed here. Pay careful attention to what tier a recipe requires - with LV machinery, MV and higher recipes are inaccessible.\nFor example, putting Clay Dust through the Electrolyzer to get Aluminium dust requires 120 EU/t (MV). Since the Basic Electrolyzer is only able to do 32EU/t, this recipe is unavailable until the Advanced Electrolyzer (MV).\nRecipes close to the voltage limit for LV (32 EU/t) may cause the machine to request multiple amps, especially if there is any cable loss. A single LV generator will often not be capable of supplying enough power if the machine is placed too far away. Most GT machines will accept up to 2 amps. A second generator or Battery Buffer may be needed to keep a a high cost recipe running. If a machine runs out of power, it will stall resetting any progress but not losing the materials. A soft mallet can be used to temporarily disable a machine to give a buffer time to recharge or wait until more power generation is available.\nSome machines require multiple amps to run at all, such as the Arc Furnace and Electric Blast Furnace . The Arc Furnace requires 3 amps to function and can accept up to 7 amps. Checking any recipe for the Arc Furnace will show Usage XX/t, voltage 32 EU/t"
- candidate chunk-6f787dc8fe247b2599f12270525e6285 doc=doc-d38e0fb2c49edead8e172a0081f57d61 sections=[Extreme Voltage (EV)] text="Extreme Voltage (EV) is the fourth electric tier in GregTech: New Horizons. In this tier, several mechanics are introduced such as Applied Energistics 2 (abbreviated as AE2) and the IndustrialCraft 2 Nuclear Reactors . This tier is largely centered around three big goals: starting an AE2 setup, traveling to Mars for new and important resources, and developing centralized electric infrastructure. It introduces several new multiblocks which greatly speed up many processes, but especially ore processing with the introduction of the Industrial Centrifuge , Industrial Maceration Stack , Ore Washing Plant , and others."
- candidate chunk-996a60daafc37f1bcf77455008b22c82 doc=doc-d8847ae5c01144e82bc225fe54375054 sections=[Zero Point Module (ZPM)] text="Zero Point Voltage (ZPM) is the seventh electric tier in GregTech: New Horizons. This tier is largely centered around building the infrastructure necessary to construct the next tier of Fusion Reactor, which is necessary for progression into UV. In addition, this tier introduces TecTech, the source of many late game multi-blocks. It also sees additional power generation with the availability of the Large Naquadah Reactor and MK2 Fusion Controller, and a new source of resources in the Void Miner . However, similar to LuV, material costs and processing requirements continue to rise and so infrastructure such as a Mega Electric Blast Furnace , Mega Vacuum Freezer , and Mega Chemical Reactor may be necessary. Circuits get a major improvement with the addition of Europium-doped wafers. The PCB Factory is also build-able at this stage, though its quest is UV. ZPM is generally considered to mark the transition from midgame to lategame."
- candidate chunk-05d33b53def3fa0645ccb3b4e9e5ffcb doc=doc-04ef7ad9e1765bcf95cbd804c813863b sections=[Ultimate Voltage (UV)] text="Ultimate Voltage (UV) is the eighth electric tier in GregTech: New Horizons. It is largely centered around the new Wetware circuit line, which introduces new multiblocks like the Quantum Computer and Research Station , as well a-s the new technologies that are unlocked with them, like the third tier of Fusion Reactor . It also introduces many multiblocks for improving infrastructure, including the Mega Alloy Blast Smelter , Component Assembly Line , and Space Elevator , as well as key automation tools like the Stocking Input Hatch."
- candidate chunk-8020be5e3374a08380c78b2c3d21be17 doc=doc-626c925b618145e4e43655f7673b36b4 sections=[Low Voltage (LV)] text="Low Voltage (LV) is the first electric tier in GregTech: New Horizons. With access to electricity comes new hazards and mechanics, but also far more variety of specialized machines that can process materials into new parts with greater efficiency."
- candidate chunk-223f6ac44ad47f65c142ca0045b909d0 doc=doc-4f4c543e0ece55aaf03e88c75a851b04 sections=[Extended Mega Ultimate Voltage (UXV)] text="Welcome to Extended Mega Ultimate Voltage (UXV) the thirteenth and final tier in GregTech: New Horizons. This tier begins with the production of Magnetohydrodynamically Constrained Star Matter (MHDCSM) and ends with a Stargate . In UXV, expect to spam even more machines and drastically increase power production to support laser energy hatches."
- candidate chunk-d76a162f5e2260296cff7570635391d4 doc=doc-0acf46d9067c834f0c19087002ed00b0 sections=[Insane Voltage (IV)] text="Insane Voltage (IV) is the fifth electric tier in GregTech: New Horizons. In this tier, multiple new multiblocks and mechanics are introduced, such as the Assembly Line , the Precise Assembler , and many more. This is also the tier where many parts of magic (Thaumcraft, etc.) are unlocked for players to use; however, as always, watch your warp effects if you have been getting warp. While transitioning from the old tier to a new tier is usually the hard part, the IV tier is by far the hardest tier to get through. If you managed to get through EV tier, prepare yourself because this tier requires a very heavy transition in almost every aspect of your base, including infrastructure and crafting. At this point, manual crafting is no longer a feasible option. With this being said, be prepared for a big change to your base!"
- candidate chunk-e53a7f7cd08cccca5de892a38320bc77 doc=doc-8666c624815c422fd88b0cdfb5cbfb2a sections=[High Voltage (HV)] text="High Voltage (HV) is the third electric tier in GregTech: New Horizons. In this tier, several new mechanics are introduced to you such as cleanrooms , space travel , and multiblock versions of your singleblock machines. With the introduction of the Cleanroom, your ability to produce more complex circuits and some of their critical components will be restricted to the inside of the Cleanroom multiblock structure, or other multiblocks that are also considered cleanrooms. Many important materials, such as titanium, can only be extracted from ores that exist on other planets and celestial bodies. You will find that all of these mechanics will carry on with you for the foreseeable future.\nUpon reaching HV, you should either have the ability to produce large quantities of stainless steel or quickly plan on developing the infrastructure to do so, as this is the first tier that starts demanding large quantities of its respective machine hull material, whether it's through the numerous single block machines or new multi-block machines such as the expensive but rewarding Distillation Tower."
- candidate chunk-15e023bdae2263e4d4d830d8dfa03301 doc=doc-8bdfdac1b12c28b48cfd59b3c35795d1 sections=[Highly Ultimate Voltage (UHV)] text="Highly Ultimate Voltage (UHV) is the ninth electric tier in GregTech: New Horizons. It unlocks Bioprocessors, infinity ingots, and Draconic Evolution. The overall goal is the construction of the Mothership and Shuttle rocket to reach the Amun-Ra system, which is required to get your foot through the door of UEV . In UHV, many previously optional processing chains and multiblocks become necessary for progression."
- candidate chunk-e6fae54548d4bafcad7b1b70ff058d85 doc=doc-0acf46d9067c834f0c19087002ed00b0 sections=[Insane Voltage (IV) > Power > Generation] text="Generation\nIn order to take full advantage of this power storage and distribution infrastructure, it is necessary for the player to also greatly expand their power generation, usually to hundreds of thousands of EU/t by the end of the tier. In order to accomplish this, IV unlocks a wealth of new power generation multiblocks, as well as processing multiblocks that greatly simplify the process of generating fuel, while also saving on power compared to their singleblock counterparts. Players using Benzene should upgrade to Nitrobenzene if they have not already, upgrade to better turbine materials such as HSS-S or HSS-E, and use new multiblocks like the Industrial Electrolyzer and Dangote Distillus . Players using oil can benefit from the Extreme Combustion Engine by upgrading to High-Octane Gasoline as their fuel, and can use the Dangote Distillus to improve the speed and efficiency of making that fuel. Nuclear Reactors remain a viable means of generating power as well, and players using steam gain access to the XL Turbo HP Steam Turbine to generate power from Superheated Steam more compactly. In addition, many unconventional means of power generation unlock in IV, such as the Deep Earth Heating Pump , Rocketdyne F-1A Engine , and Solar Tower ."
- evidence chunk-137405157af87dd0f3eee24e6971f51d doc=doc-626c925b618145e4e43655f7673b36b4 sections=[Low Voltage (LV) > GT Electric Machines] text="GT Electric Machines\nMachine GUI. Auto-output buttons circled in yellow.\nLV Electric machines have some new features that were not available with Steam-age machinery. For example, the steam exhaust is replaced with an automatic output. This output's facing can be changed with a wrench similar to how the exhaust was configured on Steam machines. Just like their Steam counterparts, all electric GT machines cannot be input or output from their front face. To enable automatic output, open the GUI of the machine and click on the button at the lower left corner. There are two automatic output toggle buttons; the left one is for liquid and the right one is for items.\nElectrolyzer clay dust recipe. The MV power requirement is circled in yellow.\nGregTech machinery is divided by tier, which corresponds to a specific voltage - LV (32 V), MV (128 V), HV (512 V) etc. Most recipes are gated to a specific voltage/tier or higher, which is shown with the recipe in NEI (Not Enough Items). If the recipe requires more than one amp it will also be listed here. Pay careful attention to what tier a recipe requires - with LV machinery, MV and higher recipes are inaccessible.\nFor example, putting Clay Dust through the Electrolyzer to get Aluminium dust requires 120 EU/t (MV). Since the Basic Electrolyzer is only able to do 32EU/t, this recipe is unavailable until the Advanced Electrolyzer (MV).\nRecipes close to the voltage limit for LV (32 EU/t) may cause the machine to request multiple amps, especially if there is any cable loss. A single LV generator will often not be capable of supplying enough power if the machine is placed too far away. Most GT machines will accept up to 2 amps. A second generator or Battery Buffer may be needed to keep a a high cost recipe running. If a machine runs out of power, it will stall resetting any progress but not losing the materials. A soft mallet can be used to temporarily disable a machine to give a buffer time to recharge or wait until more power generation is available.\nSome machines require multiple amps to run at all, such as the Arc Furnace and Electric Blast Furnace . The Arc Furnace requires 3 amps to function and can accept up to 7 amps. Checking any recipe for the Arc Furnace will show Usage XX/t, voltage 32 EU/t"

- candidate chunk-686e26db985b551ddf9bfe9947a3526c doc=doc-2dca4aebd2d58cec651996f808e3aec7 sections=[Tier] text="GTNH is divided up into tiers. Each new tier unlocks more materials, machines, multiblocks and mods available to use. The first ten tiers up to UHV are considered fully implemented, while major changes are still in development for UEV-MAX. Tier is a common term used by many mods, to represent the player's level of progress of any sort; other tiered mechanics are listed below.\nBlood Magic Altar • Coils • Electricity • Circuits • IC2 Crops • Rockets • Thaumcraft Wands\nTier | EU [ 1 ] | Casing [ 2 ] | Materials | Coil | Glass | Dimensions\n0 Stone | Wood, Stone, Flint, Leather, Coal, Charcoal, (fuel) Coke, Copper, Tin, Lapis, Redstone, Gold, Ender Pearl, Slimeball, Sticky Resin, Glue, Iron, Wrought Iron , Glass, Bronze, Lava, Water, Soularium\n1 Steam | Bronze | Nether Quartz , Sulfur, Rubber, Red Alloy , Obzinite, Obsidian [ 3 ] , Diamond, Lead [ 4 ] , Bismuth [ 5 ] , Steel | Nether [ 6 ]\n2 LV | 32 | Steel | Oil, Seed Oil, Polyethylene, Light Fuel, Refined Glue, Advanced Glue, Hydrogen, Oxygen, Helium, Nitrogen, Lithium, Sodium, Annealed Copper, Magnalium, Cupronickel, Silver [ 7 ] , Nickel [ 7 ] , Electrum, Glowstone [ 8 ] , Zinc, Cryolite, Chlorine, Damascus Steel, Vanadium, Vanadiumsteel, Molybdenum, Vis Shards, metal blocks | Cupronickel | Twilight Forest\n3 MV | 128 | Aluminium | Redstone Alloy, Cobalt, Ardite, Thaumium, Void Metal, Manasteel, Energetic Alloy, Pulsating Iron, Dark Steel, Electrical Steel, Soularium, Chrome, Silicon Solar Grade , Kanthal, IC2 Coolant, Diesel, Benzene, Stainless Steel | Kanthal\n4 HV | 512 | Stainless Steel | Vibrant Alloy, Eye of Ender [ 9 ] , Neodymium, PTFE, Radium, Radon, Rutile, Shadow Metal, Titanium | Nichrome | Glass (vanilla) | Rocket T1 Pers. Dimension Last Millenium The End [ 6 ]\n5 EV | 2k | Titanium | Tungsten, Tungstensteel, Radon, End Steel, Crystalline Alloy, Melodic Alloy, Enderium, TPV-Alloy, HSS-G, Platinum, Iridium, Terrasteel | TPV-Alloy | Managlass Reinforced Glass TRB Glass Block [ 10 ] | Rocket T2, Spectre\n6 IV | 8k | Tungstensteel | Naquadah, Stellar Alloy, Osmium, HSS-S, Elementium, Gaia Spirit, Rhodium, Palladium | HSS-G HSS-S | Alfglass TsRB Glass Block [ 11 ] Thorium Yttrium Glass Block | Rocket T3\n7 LuV | 32k | Rhodium-Plated Palladium | Europium | Naquadah Naquadah Alloy | RPPRB Glass Block [ 12 ]"
- candidate chunk-b33c0a67b59566f01a7953640ba68dd4 doc=doc-a9f4f7813df646e0e5f0ab08ab29b50e sections=[Ludicrous Voltage (LuV) > Progression] text="Progression\nLuV is a much smaller tier compared to IV in terms of different milestones. The capstone of this tier is the Mk I. Fusion Reactor; it enables crafting Europium, which in turn gates T5 rockets, europium-doped wafers, the Crystal circuit line, and more."
- candidate chunk-c4f515f28759c9259bd668bf7140e214 doc=doc-a9f4f7813df646e0e5f0ab08ab29b50e sections=[Ludicrous Voltage (LuV)] text="Ludicrous Voltage (LuV) is the sixth electric tier in GregTech: New Horizons. This tier is largely centered around building the infrastructure necessary to construct the first tier of Fusion Reactor , as well as the new items and machines gated by fusion. This is the first tier where the Assembly Line is used to craft on-tier components as well as highly advanced machines. In addition, the costs and crafting trees for machines are even larger than in previous tiers, making fully automated on-demand crafting with AE2 absolutely necessary. The massive demand for robust infrastructure continues for the rest of the game; arrays of Volcanus consuming on-tier voltage are highly suggested."
- candidate chunk-692a2c16b62a6206e99975eb0147e9f6 doc=doc-709fa05c9dc5e6bdd1e4979266413ad2 sections=[Power Generation > Steam] text="Steam\nSteam is the first power source you have access to, and is the fuel for the first Bronze and Steel machines. Upon reaching LV , it is also the easiest way to generate EU, but boilers rapidly become less useful in the following tiers due to how difficult it becomes to transport a lot of Steam around to match the EU/t demands of your upgraded machines, especially your Electric Blast Furnace. After players centralise their power generation (usually around EV - IV) and no longer need to transport \"fuel\" around their base, Steam starts to become increasingly useful in power generation again, especially as they gain access to superheated, supercritical or dense variants of Steam."
- candidate chunk-8020be5e3374a08380c78b2c3d21be17 doc=doc-626c925b618145e4e43655f7673b36b4 sections=[Low Voltage (LV)] text="Low Voltage (LV) is the first electric tier in GregTech: New Horizons. With access to electricity comes new hazards and mechanics, but also far more variety of specialized machines that can process materials into new parts with greater efficiency."
- candidate chunk-4ebba377064a29e9d4a2afac5f24e1a7 doc=doc-d8847ae5c01144e82bc225fe54375054 sections=[Zero Point Module (ZPM) > Infrastructure Improvements > Power Generation] text="Power Generation\nIn ZPM, most existing power generation methods from earlier tiers, such as Nitrobenzene , Rocket Fuel, and Nuclear Reactors , remain viable, but start to fall off due to increasing power demands and a lack of upgrades. One notable upgrade is Huge Turbines for all gas, steam, or plasma-based power, which are unlocked with Americium.\nPlasma, on the other hand, is still viable and will continue to be viable for many more tiers. In addition to the aforementioned Huge Turbines, plasma benefits from better fuels in the form of Tin Plasma or Runite Plasma (although the latter requires a UV Alloy Blast Smelter ), better production rates using the Mk2 Fusion Reactor, and the XL Plasma Turbine and XL Supercritical Steam Turbine to greatly improve power throughput.\nA Large Naquadah Reactor.\nZPM also unlocks one of the strongest power generation methods in the mid and late game: Naquadah Fuel, often abbreviated to Naqfuel. Power is produced by inputting Naqfuel into a Large Naquadah Reactor (LNR), alongside Liquid Air, a coolant (Gelid Cryotheum), and an excited liquid (Molten Atomic Separation Catalyst), all of which will need to be produced in large quantities by players seeking to use the fuel for power. Two tiers of Naqfuel are available in ZPM: tier 1 requires the Mk2 Fusion Reactor, while tier 2 is produced by combining tier 1 fuel with a couple other ingredients in an overclocked Mega Chemical Reactor . Each LNR can generate up to 42.9M EU/t with Naqfuel 1, and up to 101.2M EU/t with Naqfuel 2."
- candidate chunk-517bbd1b26feff56dfdafe2f4c8c18fe doc=doc-626c925b618145e4e43655f7673b36b4 sections=[Low Voltage (LV) > Goals] text="Goals\nSome of the primary milestones for this tier are as follows:\n- Batch craft Electronic Circuits\n- LV components - Motor, Piston, Pump, Robot Arm, Sensor, Emitter\n- LV Machines - Bending Machine (priority), Wiremill (priority), Lathe, Assembler, Electrolyzer, Chemical Reactor, Fluid Extractor, etc.\n- Acquire Lithium and Lead for Batteries\n- Pump Oil for Polyethylene and Light Fuel\n- Upgrade Power Generation\n- Build Good Circuits\n- Locate Mica (or make the more expensive Alumino Silicate Wool)\n- Construct an Electric Blast Furnace & maintenance tools to get Aluminum\nOptional Side Quests:\n- Increase Hearts by eating a variety of foods\n- Plan for a larger base to accommodate MV machines / multiblocks\n- Make multiple Backpacks (Forestry/ModdedNetwork)\n- Hang Glider , Piston Boots , Healing Axe (if not already acquired)\n- Visit the Twilight Forest\n- More storage with drawer/barrel upgrades, Diamond Chests\n- Sound Mufflers, Chandeliers\n- Tool & Armor Upgrades\n- Item Dislocator (or another Magnet )"
- candidate chunk-bd30c29740c8b96a881cb63d33fa8578 doc=doc-0acf46d9067c834f0c19087002ed00b0 sections=[Insane Voltage (IV) > Progression Goals > Rockets] text="Rockets\nUnlike most tiers, IV contains two different tiers of rockets and associated sets of planets that they unlock. The Tier 3 Rocket is available early in the tier, only being gated behind the IV Assembling Machine to make some of the parts. The Tier 4 Rocket, on the other hand, requires the Assembly Line, meaning it is unavailable until just before the start of LuV. In addition, both tiers require an upgraded rocket fuel, Dense Hydrazine, in order to use, which will require building a Chemical Plant for players who opted to use Cetane-Boosted Diesel for the first two tiers. Most planets from Tier 3 onwards also require an upgraded set of Thermal Padding, which needs PBI to make, and the Tier 4 planet Venus requires a full set of armor with the SpaceSuit property. For a full list of resources available on the Tier 3 and 4 planets, see the Planets page. The Tier 3 rocket also unlocks the ability to make a space station over Jupiter, although the Tier 4 rocket is required to make the Planetary Gas Siphon that makes such a space station useful."
- candidate chunk-64ac91fc27b4dab9433816ad4cfe166a doc=doc-626c925b618145e4e43655f7673b36b4 sections=[Low Voltage (LV) > Revisiting Steam > Steam Power Upgrades] text="Steam Power Upgrades\nUp until LV your most automated options for generating Steam involved either regularly resetting Solar Boilers or collecting wood manually to feed Coke Ovens, the LV Crop Manager and tree bonsais allow for easy and cheap automation of Wood, enabling fully automatic power. Look into powering it with a semifluid generator directly with creosote, its a bit more power efficient and saves on pipe distance in most builds. You can try and breed the bonsais for max stats but that won't be necessary, even one 11x11 layer of 1/1/1 bonsais will provide more than enough wood for a max size RC Boiler . Speaking of RC boilers, the tank recipe gets substantially cheaper in LV, with the screw requirement being dropped with the assembler; the casing required for the tanks can also be made cheaper by utilising the Bender and the Cutting Machine.\nAfter visiting the Twilight Forest, the player can utilise the lead found to make Potin fluid pipes, which offer substantially more throughput than Bronze pipes of the same size. You can utilise the Fluid Extractor and Solidifier to produce pipes substantially quicker than with the Bender."
- candidate chunk-ae55214ebef9ffae0724fb9e54ef0116 doc=doc-709fa05c9dc5e6bdd1e4979266413ad2 sections=[Power Generation > Steam > Production > Later Tier Steam Production] text="Later Tier Steam Production\nIn later tiers, large quantities of Supercritical or Superheated Steam can be produced by a Large Heat Exchanger fed by hot fluids like lava or hot coolant, which is able to be produced infinitely in several ways (e.g Everburn Urns from Thaumcraft). Another way to get Superheated Steam is through the Large Titanium Boiler and Large Tungstensteel Boiler, but run only with more specific fuels (like Solid Super Fuels). This continues into the endgame, with Dense Supercritical Steam being provided by an Extreme Heat Exchanger . Many mid- to late-game power generation options use steam in some capacity, whether directly, or via a heat exchanger (e.g. the Fusion Reactor , Solar Tower Fluid Reactors , DEHP , Solid-Oxide Fuel Cells , HTGR , LFTR , THTR )."

Answer status: insufficient_context
Refusal: true
Answer: The supplied evidence does not establish a complete list of unsafe power connections to avoid while progressing. It only describes general cable behavior: cables can catch fire and melt if they receive amps containing more EU than their maximum Voltage, or if more amps travel through them than their maximum Amperage limit, and each energy packet loses Voltage per block travelled according to Loss/Meter/Ampere. It also gives one example that a 1x Tin Cable can handle 1A containing 32EU at a loss of 1 EU-Volt/Meter/Ampere, and notes that uninsulated wires generally have twice the loss of insulated cables. However, the evidence does not provide the full set of unsafe connection practices, tier-by-tier cable limits, or progression-specific hazards needed to answer the question completely.

- candidate chunk-dd68c611d886bf93d3589f0e90126008 doc=doc-3dbc9b7b97cfc0b8fbe14bc95a57ccc0 sections=[Electricity > Trivia] text="Trivia\n- GregTech's current power system is called the GT Enet API (Enet).\n- Since version 5.0 (for Minecraft 1.7.2), GregTech has its own Energy System since GregoriusT wasn't satisfied with IC2 Experimental's Energy System.\nThe reasons of why I removed compatibility to the IC² Enet are that Cable Loss doesn't work, that the Network doesn't have Packets anymore and that it switched from Integer to Double (what is horrible for larger Energy Storages). Not to mention that it is very hard to have control over Energy flow without constantly registering and unregistering TileEntities. — GregoriusT"
- candidate chunk-d76a162f5e2260296cff7570635391d4 doc=doc-0acf46d9067c834f0c19087002ed00b0 sections=[Insane Voltage (IV)] text="Insane Voltage (IV) is the fifth electric tier in GregTech: New Horizons. In this tier, multiple new multiblocks and mechanics are introduced, such as the Assembly Line , the Precise Assembler , and many more. This is also the tier where many parts of magic (Thaumcraft, etc.) are unlocked for players to use; however, as always, watch your warp effects if you have been getting warp. While transitioning from the old tier to a new tier is usually the hard part, the IV tier is by far the hardest tier to get through. If you managed to get through EV tier, prepare yourself because this tier requires a very heavy transition in almost every aspect of your base, including infrastructure and crafting. At this point, manual crafting is no longer a feasible option. With this being said, be prepared for a big change to your base!"
- candidate chunk-fdf371b1592b7ed0250bf63b741b8cb9 doc=doc-3dbc9b7b97cfc0b8fbe14bc95a57ccc0 sections=[Electricity > Cables and Loss] text="Cables and Loss\nGregTech EU can be transferred using GregTech Cables.\nRather than priority by destination, power priority is given by cardinal direction. Cables will push power in the following priority:\n- Down\n- Up\n- North\n- South\n- West\n- East\nWhen producing power, each generator will always attempt to empty all of its internal buffer at once. This can cause one generator in a cable network to use fuel more rapidly than another, with order depending on tile entity processing and changing on chunk reloads.\nAll GT Cables have a max Voltage, max Amperage and a Loss/Meter/Ampere, indicated on their tooltip:\n- Cables which receive amps containing more EU than their maximum Voltage will catch fire and melt.\n- Cables that have more amps travelling through them than their maximum Amperage limit will catch fire and melt. Note that machines can request more Amperage than strictly required by their active recipe.\n- Each energy packet travelling through a cable loses Voltage per block travelled. The amount of EU lost is indicated by the Loss/Meter/Ampere number.\nEach Material has 1x, 2x, 4x, 8x, 12x, and 16x uninsulated Wires. Most of these have cable variants, except for the superconductor wires, as well as graphene, Ichorium, hypogen, Black Plutonium, Awakened Draconium, Infinity, and SpaceTime wires. Note that Uninsulated Wires have twice the Loss/Meter/Ampere as Insulated Cables. The only exceptions to this are Red alloy and Redstone alloy (as their cables are lossless, while the wires are not), Quantium, Lumiium, and Signalium (four times the loss when uninsulated), and Bedrockium (8x loss when uninsulated).\nFor example:\n- A 1x Tin Cable can handle 1A containing 32EU at a loss of 1 EU-Volt/Meter/Ampere. This means that 1 amp can travel 32 blocks before it dies.\n- A 1x Tin Wire can handle 1A containing 32EU at a loss of 2 EU-Volt/Meter/Ampere. In this case, the amp can travel 16 blocks only.\nBelow is a table of the current properties of various types of cables in GregTech: Note: In 2.8.0, many cables received maximum amperage and loss changes. This table is accurate as of version 2.8.1+.\nMaterial | Max Voltage [ 1 ] | Amp [ 2 ] | Loss [ 3 ] | Decay [ 4 ]\nMaterial: Tin | Max Voltage [ 1 ]: LV (32) | Amp [ 2 ]: 1 | Loss [ 3 ]: 1 | Decay ["
- candidate chunk-8020be5e3374a08380c78b2c3d21be17 doc=doc-626c925b618145e4e43655f7673b36b4 sections=[Low Voltage (LV)] text="Low Voltage (LV) is the first electric tier in GregTech: New Horizons. With access to electricity comes new hazards and mechanics, but also far more variety of specialized machines that can process materials into new parts with greater efficiency."
- candidate chunk-05d33b53def3fa0645ccb3b4e9e5ffcb doc=doc-04ef7ad9e1765bcf95cbd804c813863b sections=[Ultimate Voltage (UV)] text="Ultimate Voltage (UV) is the eighth electric tier in GregTech: New Horizons. It is largely centered around the new Wetware circuit line, which introduces new multiblocks like the Quantum Computer and Research Station , as well a-s the new technologies that are unlocked with them, like the third tier of Fusion Reactor . It also introduces many multiblocks for improving infrastructure, including the Mega Alloy Blast Smelter , Component Assembly Line , and Space Elevator , as well as key automation tools like the Stocking Input Hatch."
- candidate chunk-996a60daafc37f1bcf77455008b22c82 doc=doc-d8847ae5c01144e82bc225fe54375054 sections=[Zero Point Module (ZPM)] text="Zero Point Voltage (ZPM) is the seventh electric tier in GregTech: New Horizons. This tier is largely centered around building the infrastructure necessary to construct the next tier of Fusion Reactor, which is necessary for progression into UV. In addition, this tier introduces TecTech, the source of many late game multi-blocks. It also sees additional power generation with the availability of the Large Naquadah Reactor and MK2 Fusion Controller, and a new source of resources in the Void Miner . However, similar to LuV, material costs and processing requirements continue to rise and so infrastructure such as a Mega Electric Blast Furnace , Mega Vacuum Freezer , and Mega Chemical Reactor may be necessary. Circuits get a major improvement with the addition of Europium-doped wafers. The PCB Factory is also build-able at this stage, though its quest is UV. ZPM is generally considered to mark the transition from midgame to lategame."
- candidate chunk-6f787dc8fe247b2599f12270525e6285 doc=doc-d38e0fb2c49edead8e172a0081f57d61 sections=[Extreme Voltage (EV)] text="Extreme Voltage (EV) is the fourth electric tier in GregTech: New Horizons. In this tier, several mechanics are introduced such as Applied Energistics 2 (abbreviated as AE2) and the IndustrialCraft 2 Nuclear Reactors . This tier is largely centered around three big goals: starting an AE2 setup, traveling to Mars for new and important resources, and developing centralized electric infrastructure. It introduces several new multiblocks which greatly speed up many processes, but especially ore processing with the introduction of the Industrial Centrifuge , Industrial Maceration Stack , Ore Washing Plant , and others."
- candidate chunk-e53a7f7cd08cccca5de892a38320bc77 doc=doc-8666c624815c422fd88b0cdfb5cbfb2a sections=[High Voltage (HV)] text="High Voltage (HV) is the third electric tier in GregTech: New Horizons. In this tier, several new mechanics are introduced to you such as cleanrooms , space travel , and multiblock versions of your singleblock machines. With the introduction of the Cleanroom, your ability to produce more complex circuits and some of their critical components will be restricted to the inside of the Cleanroom multiblock structure, or other multiblocks that are also considered cleanrooms. Many important materials, such as titanium, can only be extracted from ores that exist on other planets and celestial bodies. You will find that all of these mechanics will carry on with you for the foreseeable future.\nUpon reaching HV, you should either have the ability to produce large quantities of stainless steel or quickly plan on developing the infrastructure to do so, as this is the first tier that starts demanding large quantities of its respective machine hull material, whether it's through the numerous single block machines or new multi-block machines such as the expensive but rewarding Distillation Tower."
- candidate chunk-19b76a2f4b654304a4b109eb577489fa doc=doc-626c925b618145e4e43655f7673b36b4 sections=[Low Voltage (LV) > Energy Conversion > GT EU to Others] text="GT EU to Others\nGT Cables can be attached to almost any machine that requires some kind of energy, allowing a single power network to run machines from most mods in the pack including IC2, EnderIO, Forestry, Railcraft, Galacticraft and more. Due to the idiosyncrasies of ENet, at least one GT machine (a machine hull will do the job nicely) has to be present for power to transfer on GT cables/wires. Placing the cable last sometimes also helps the setup register as valid."
- candidate chunk-223f6ac44ad47f65c142ca0045b909d0 doc=doc-4f4c543e0ece55aaf03e88c75a851b04 sections=[Extended Mega Ultimate Voltage (UXV)] text="Welcome to Extended Mega Ultimate Voltage (UXV) the thirteenth and final tier in GregTech: New Horizons. This tier begins with the production of Magnetohydrodynamically Constrained Star Matter (MHDCSM) and ends with a Stargate . In UXV, expect to spam even more machines and drastically increase power production to support laser energy hatches."

### Lane 2 — memory=true

Answer status: insufficient_context
Refusal: true
Answer: The supplied evidence does not identify any unsafe power connections to avoid while progressing. It only describes steam as a power source and mentions later-tier steam production methods; it contains no warnings or guidance about unsafe power connections. Please provide the relevant power-connection safety evidence.
Goal state: "Plan and take a first trip to the Moon at LV tier in GT:NH" (quote "I am at LV and want my first Moon trip.")
Clarifications: `[{Key:current_tier Value:LV Quote:I am at LV} {Key:current_power Value:steam power Quote:I currently have steam power.}]`

- candidate chunk-33ead29e8cd8bacf04be05f96c988afe doc=doc-8666c624815c422fd88b0cdfb5cbfb2a sections=[High Voltage (HV) > Progression > Preparation for making the rocket] text="Preparation for making the rocket\nYou need a NASA workbench and a Implosion Compressor for your first rocket. You will find that the NASA Workbench is a particularly arduous craft that for some reason requires you to use GT tools in a crafting grid in HV. On the other hand, the Implosion Compressor should be trivial for the average player, regardless of setup.\nThe amount of explosives needed for use with the Implosion Compressor is non-trivial, though. You will be expending several stacks of TNT. If your energy setup is based on benzene, you will find that obtaining enough toluene for TNT will be trivial if you upgrade your setup to use a Distillation Tower instead of single-block distilleries. However, if your energy setup is based on oil, you will need some additional processing steps-- diverting some Heavy Fuel for use in a distillery or Distillation Tower will likely be the best way.\nYou'll need 37 T1 heavy-duty alloy plates and a ton of additional materials. Check the quest book. You'll need a T1 rocket, an oxygen equipment set with 2 light oxygen tanks (only 1 is very risky for resource collecting), an oxygen collector and compressor, a fuel loader, and 9 landing pads."
- candidate chunk-692a2c16b62a6206e99975eb0147e9f6 doc=doc-709fa05c9dc5e6bdd1e4979266413ad2 sections=[Power Generation > Steam] text="Steam\nSteam is the first power source you have access to, and is the fuel for the first Bronze and Steel machines. Upon reaching LV , it is also the easiest way to generate EU, but boilers rapidly become less useful in the following tiers due to how difficult it becomes to transport a lot of Steam around to match the EU/t demands of your upgraded machines, especially your Electric Blast Furnace. After players centralise their power generation (usually around EV - IV) and no longer need to transport \"fuel\" around their base, Steam starts to become increasingly useful in power generation again, especially as they gain access to superheated, supercritical or dense variants of Steam."
- candidate chunk-ae55214ebef9ffae0724fb9e54ef0116 doc=doc-709fa05c9dc5e6bdd1e4979266413ad2 sections=[Power Generation > Steam > Production > Later Tier Steam Production] text="Later Tier Steam Production\nIn later tiers, large quantities of Supercritical or Superheated Steam can be produced by a Large Heat Exchanger fed by hot fluids like lava or hot coolant, which is able to be produced infinitely in several ways (e.g Everburn Urns from Thaumcraft). Another way to get Superheated Steam is through the Large Titanium Boiler and Large Tungstensteel Boiler, but run only with more specific fuels (like Solid Super Fuels). This continues into the endgame, with Dense Supercritical Steam being provided by an Extreme Heat Exchanger . Many mid- to late-game power generation options use steam in some capacity, whether directly, or via a heat exchanger (e.g. the Fusion Reactor , Solar Tower Fluid Reactors , DEHP , Solid-Oxide Fuel Cells , HTGR , LFTR , THTR )."
- candidate chunk-a5f28cb4868a57c9395131f83cfe3cd6 doc=doc-d38e0fb2c49edead8e172a0081f57d61 sections=[Extreme Voltage (EV) > Progression > Your second rocket / Late-EV] text="Your second rocket / Late-EV\nAfter getting the EV field generator you needed for the rocket computer, it's time to build your second rocket. It's a pretty straightforward step. If you forgot or didn't get much meteoric iron, you have to get back to the moon and mine some. I recommend you to have 12 stacks or 45 stacks if you don't want to go back to the moon again for meteoric iron (also for t3 rocket). With the exception of several new parts, which are booster and t2 landing module, the rest goes the same as t1 rocket but is more expensive.\nYou only need a trip to mars for you to get all the resources needed. Also, T2 lander doesn't need you to slow down when approaching as it's a balloon that bounces after it reaches the surface. Your mission there is to collect desh vein (it also has tungsten ore), tungsten vein (also provides lithium), and pitchblende vein (also provides thorium) if you didn't get enough uranium by centrifuging thorium ore. Remember to explore mars more for finding the mars dungeon which contains the T3 rocket blueprint."
- candidate chunk-14d878ee8526ed79eb222ab4862439f1 doc=doc-8666c624815c422fd88b0cdfb5cbfb2a sections=[High Voltage (HV) > Progression > Blast Off \O/] text="Blast Off \\O/\nYou did it! You crafted your first rocket ever in this modpack. Now you need to fuel it with 5 cells worth of cetane-boosted diesel. Note that rocket will produce a huge amount of pollution if launched. When you land, you should find yourself in a lander. YOU MUST HOLD THE SPACE BAR (or you will die and lose your rocket) to slow down the lander. You should keep your descent velocity to less than 20 m/s to avoid exploding yourself on landing. Try to find the Ilmenite vein (good source of Titanium and Chrome), Bauxite (for Ilmenite and a lot of Aluminium), and Neodymium (for EV+ motors, Fluorine, Phosphorus, and Rare Earth elements that you need later in the game)."
- candidate chunk-4ebba377064a29e9d4a2afac5f24e1a7 doc=doc-d8847ae5c01144e82bc225fe54375054 sections=[Zero Point Module (ZPM) > Infrastructure Improvements > Power Generation] text="Power Generation\nIn ZPM, most existing power generation methods from earlier tiers, such as Nitrobenzene , Rocket Fuel, and Nuclear Reactors , remain viable, but start to fall off due to increasing power demands and a lack of upgrades. One notable upgrade is Huge Turbines for all gas, steam, or plasma-based power, which are unlocked with Americium.\nPlasma, on the other hand, is still viable and will continue to be viable for many more tiers. In addition to the aforementioned Huge Turbines, plasma benefits from better fuels in the form of Tin Plasma or Runite Plasma (although the latter requires a UV Alloy Blast Smelter ), better production rates using the Mk2 Fusion Reactor, and the XL Plasma Turbine and XL Supercritical Steam Turbine to greatly improve power throughput.\nA Large Naquadah Reactor.\nZPM also unlocks one of the strongest power generation methods in the mid and late game: Naquadah Fuel, often abbreviated to Naqfuel. Power is produced by inputting Naqfuel into a Large Naquadah Reactor (LNR), alongside Liquid Air, a coolant (Gelid Cryotheum), and an excited liquid (Molten Atomic Separation Catalyst), all of which will need to be produced in large quantities by players seeking to use the fuel for power. Two tiers of Naqfuel are available in ZPM: tier 1 requires the Mk2 Fusion Reactor, while tier 2 is produced by combining tier 1 fuel with a couple other ingredients in an overclocked Mega Chemical Reactor . Each LNR can generate up to 42.9M EU/t with Naqfuel 1, and up to 101.2M EU/t with Naqfuel 2."
- candidate chunk-281335ed0efda71a27ab652405366a1b doc=doc-04ef7ad9e1765bcf95cbd804c813863b sections=[Ultimate Voltage (UV) > Infrastructure Improvements > Power] text="Power\nUV is the point at which power generation from the early game falls off: Nitrobenzene, Rocket Fuel, and Nuclear Reactors can last up to this point, but should be replaced by the start of the next tier. Plasma or Naquadah Fuel, both of which are viable in UV and able to scale for multiple more tiers, should be used instead. Plasma gains access to the Fusion Reactor Mk3, which provides both access to the best plasma chain fuels and an overclock on earlier fusion recipes to reduce the need for spam, and the Stocking Input Hatch helps parallelize on slower fusion steps. It also benefits from new better turbine materials, such as Cosmic Neutronium. Naquadah Fuel also benefits from these improvements for its slow fusion step, and gains access to Haumea Void Miners , the Space Elevator, and the Mega Apiary to help source its inputs."
- candidate chunk-e6fae54548d4bafcad7b1b70ff058d85 doc=doc-0acf46d9067c834f0c19087002ed00b0 sections=[Insane Voltage (IV) > Power > Generation] text="Generation\nIn order to take full advantage of this power storage and distribution infrastructure, it is necessary for the player to also greatly expand their power generation, usually to hundreds of thousands of EU/t by the end of the tier. In order to accomplish this, IV unlocks a wealth of new power generation multiblocks, as well as processing multiblocks that greatly simplify the process of generating fuel, while also saving on power compared to their singleblock counterparts. Players using Benzene should upgrade to Nitrobenzene if they have not already, upgrade to better turbine materials such as HSS-S or HSS-E, and use new multiblocks like the Industrial Electrolyzer and Dangote Distillus . Players using oil can benefit from the Extreme Combustion Engine by upgrading to High-Octane Gasoline as their fuel, and can use the Dangote Distillus to improve the speed and efficiency of making that fuel. Nuclear Reactors remain a viable means of generating power as well, and players using steam gain access to the XL Turbo HP Steam Turbine to generate power from Superheated Steam more compactly. In addition, many unconventional means of power generation unlock in IV, such as the Deep Earth Heating Pump , Rocketdyne F-1A Engine , and Solar Tower ."
- candidate chunk-64ac91fc27b4dab9433816ad4cfe166a doc=doc-626c925b618145e4e43655f7673b36b4 sections=[Low Voltage (LV) > Revisiting Steam > Steam Power Upgrades] text="Steam Power Upgrades\nUp until LV your most automated options for generating Steam involved either regularly resetting Solar Boilers or collecting wood manually to feed Coke Ovens, the LV Crop Manager and tree bonsais allow for easy and cheap automation of Wood, enabling fully automatic power. Look into powering it with a semifluid generator directly with creosote, its a bit more power efficient and saves on pipe distance in most builds. You can try and breed the bonsais for max stats but that won't be necessary, even one 11x11 layer of 1/1/1 bonsais will provide more than enough wood for a max size RC Boiler . Speaking of RC boilers, the tank recipe gets substantially cheaper in LV, with the screw requirement being dropped with the assembler; the casing required for the tanks can also be made cheaper by utilising the Bender and the Cutting Machine.\nAfter visiting the Twilight Forest, the player can utilise the lead found to make Potin fluid pipes, which offer substantially more throughput than Bronze pipes of the same size. You can utilise the Fluid Extractor and Solidifier to produce pipes substantially quicker than with the Bender."
- candidate chunk-8c0401ed3a60ab21dc1e43801523f528 doc=doc-0b20024c57c212406bf68b660f2eaf30 sections=[Rockets > Crafting a Tier 1 Rocket > Storage Rockets] text="Storage Rockets\nIn the T1 Rocket craft, there is an optional slot below the rocket control computer to add a copper, iron, or gold chest into (these recipes are also available in NEI ). This will add storage space to the rocket, but it will not be detected by the T1 Rocket quest in the Space Race tab. These can be converted to the normal rocket in a crafting grid."

Upon reaching LV , it is also the easiest way to generate EU, but boilers rapidly become less useful in the following tiers due to how difficult it becomes to transport a lot of Steam around to match the EU/t demands of your upgraded machines, especially your Electric Blast Furnace. After players centralise their power generation (usually around EV - IV) and no longer need to transport \"fuel\" around their base, Steam starts to become increasingly useful in power generation again, especially as they gain access to superheated, supercritical or dense variants of Steam."
- evidence chunk-ae55214ebef9ffae0724fb9e54ef0116 doc=doc-709fa05c9dc5e6bdd1e4979266413ad2 sections=[Power Generation > Steam > Production > Later Tier Steam Production] text="Later Tier Steam Production\nIn later tiers, large quantities of Supercritical or Superheated Steam can be produced by a Large Heat Exchanger fed by hot fluids like lava or hot coolant, which is able to be produced infinitely in several ways (e.g Everburn Urns from Thaumcraft). Another way to get Superheated Steam is through the Large Titanium Boiler and Large Tungstensteel Boiler, but run only with more specific fuels (like Solid Super Fuels). This continues into the endgame, with Dense Supercritical Steam being provided by an Extreme Heat Exchanger . Many mid- to late-game power generation options use steam in some capacity, whether directly, or via a heat exchanger (e.g. the Fusion Reactor , Solar Tower Fluid Reactors , DEHP , Solid-Oxide Fuel Cells , HTGR , LFTR , THTR )."

- candidate chunk-fd0e7d7c4b95cf731b2074dbbab5e234 doc=doc-16afb764b518d80604353589d360da21 sections=[Medium Voltage (MV)] text="Medium Voltage (MV) is the second electric tier in GregTech: New Horizons. It introduces fewer new mechanics, but instead improves and expands what the player has already seen in LV ."
- candidate chunk-996a60daafc37f1bcf77455008b22c82 doc=doc-d8847ae5c01144e82bc225fe54375054 sections=[Zero Point Module (ZPM)] text="Zero Point Voltage (ZPM) is the seventh electric tier in GregTech: New Horizons. This tier is largely centered around building the infrastructure necessary to construct the next tier of Fusion Reactor, which is necessary for progression into UV. In addition, this tier introduces TecTech, the source of many late game multi-blocks. It also sees additional power generation with the availability of the Large Naquadah Reactor and MK2 Fusion Controller, and a new source of resources in the Void Miner . However, similar to LuV, material costs and processing requirements continue to rise and so infrastructure such as a Mega Electric Blast Furnace , Mega Vacuum Freezer , and Mega Chemical Reactor may be necessary. Circuits get a major improvement with the addition of Europium-doped wafers. The PCB Factory is also build-able at this stage, though its quest is UV. ZPM is generally considered to mark the transition from midgame to lategame."
- candidate chunk-05d33b53def3fa0645ccb3b4e9e5ffcb doc=doc-04ef7ad9e1765bcf95cbd804c813863b sections=[Ultimate Voltage (UV)] text="Ultimate Voltage (UV) is the eighth electric tier in GregTech: New Horizons. It is largely centered around the new Wetware circuit line, which introduces new multiblocks like the Quantum Computer and Research Station , as well a-s the new technologies that are unlocked with them, like the third tier of Fusion Reactor . It also introduces many multiblocks for improving infrastructure, including the Mega Alloy Blast Smelter , Component Assembly Line , and Space Elevator , as well as key automation tools like the Stocking Input Hatch."
- candidate chunk-8020be5e3374a08380c78b2c3d21be17 doc=doc-626c925b618145e4e43655f7673b36b4 sections=[Low Voltage (LV)] text="Low Voltage (LV) is the first electric tier in GregTech: New Horizons. With access to electricity comes new hazards and mechanics, but also far more variety of specialized machines that can process materials into new parts with greater efficiency."
- candidate chunk-a5f28cb4868a57c9395131f83cfe3cd6 doc=doc-d38e0fb2c49edead8e172a0081f57d61 sections=[Extreme Voltage (EV) > Progression > Your second rocket / Late-EV] text="Your second rocket / Late-EV\nAfter getting the EV field generator you needed for the rocket computer, it's time to build your second rocket. It's a pretty straightforward step. If you forgot or didn't get much meteoric iron, you have to get back to the moon and mine some. I recommend you to have 12 stacks or 45 stacks if you don't want to go back to the moon again for meteoric iron (also for t3 rocket). With the exception of several new parts, which are booster and t2 landing module, the rest goes the same as t1 rocket but is more expensive.\nYou only need a trip to mars for you to get all the resources needed. Also, T2 lander doesn't need you to slow down when approaching as it's a balloon that bounces after it reaches the surface. Your mission there is to collect desh vein (it also has tungsten ore), tungsten vein (also provides lithium), and pitchblende vein (also provides thorium) if you didn't get enough uranium by centrifuging thorium ore. Remember to explore mars more for finding the mars dungeon which contains the T3 rocket blueprint."
- candidate chunk-c18096e8b035aa08daa337f0ba995048 doc=doc-8bdfdac1b12c28b48cfd59b3c35795d1 sections=[Highly Ultimate Voltage (UHV) > Progression > Mothership] text="Mothership\nThe Mothership is the end of UHV progression, being required to reach Tier 9 planets and obtain Tengam, the magnetic material in UEV through UMV motors. Creating a Mothership requires launching a Shuttle Rocket while holding the necessary blocks and items, similar to a space station. The Shuttle Rocket's schematic is not found in a dungeon like previous schematics, but is instead crafted in the Assembly Line. Once the Mothership is built, engines must be placed on it and provided with fuel to travel to the Amun-Ra system, which contains the six Tier 9 planets; the only relevant to unlock UEV is Seth, which contains Tengam, as the Space Elevator is a better source of everything else on these planets. A small amount of dilithium from Neper is useful early in UEV. The Space Elevator can be used to travel to them, but only after obtaining MK-III motors, which require the DTPF. The Mothership also requires many previously optional steps in progression, such as the Nano Forge , Radox, and Kevlar."
- candidate chunk-6f787dc8fe247b2599f12270525e6285 doc=doc-d38e0fb2c49edead8e172a0081f57d61 sections=[Extreme Voltage (EV)] text="Extreme Voltage (EV) is the fourth electric tier in GregTech: New Horizons. In this tier, several mechanics are introduced such as Applied Energistics 2 (abbreviated as AE2) and the IndustrialCraft 2 Nuclear Reactors . This tier is largely centered around three big goals: starting an AE2 setup, traveling to Mars for new and important resources, and developing centralized electric infrastructure. It introduces several new multiblocks which greatly speed up many processes, but especially ore processing with the introduction of the Industrial Centrifuge , Industrial Maceration Stack , Ore Washing Plant , and others."
- candidate chunk-15e023bdae2263e4d4d830d8dfa03301 doc=doc-8bdfdac1b12c28b48cfd59b3c35795d1 sections=[Highly Ultimate Voltage (UHV)] text="Highly Ultimate Voltage (UHV) is the ninth electric tier in GregTech: New Horizons. It unlocks Bioprocessors, infinity ingots, and Draconic Evolution. The overall goal is the construction of the Mothership and Shuttle rocket to reach the Amun-Ra system, which is required to get your foot through the door of UEV . In UHV, many previously optional processing chains and multiblocks become necessary for progression."
- candidate chunk-9789157d3151f95d6483f88ec29e24bb doc=doc-0b20024c57c212406bf68b660f2eaf30 sections=[Rockets] text="A Tier 1 Rocket, on launch pad\nThe Rocket is a vehicle added by the Galacticraft mod that enables travel to different planets . These planets often contain ore veins and underground fluid veins which are inaccessible in other dimensions and are required for Tier progression."

- candidate chunk-686e26db985b551ddf9bfe9947a3526c doc=doc-2dca4aebd2d58cec651996f808e3aec7 sections=[Tier] text="GTNH is divided up into tiers. Each new tier unlocks more materials, machines, multiblocks and mods available to use. The first ten tiers up to UHV are considered fully implemented, while major changes are still in development for UEV-MAX. Tier is a common term used by many mods, to represent the player's level of progress of any sort; other tiered mechanics are listed below.\nBlood Magic Altar • Coils • Electricity • Circuits • IC2 Crops • Rockets • Thaumcraft Wands\nTier | EU [ 1 ] | Casing [ 2 ] | Materials | Coil | Glass | Dimensions\n0 Stone | Wood, Stone, Flint, Leather, Coal, Charcoal, (fuel) Coke, Copper, Tin, Lapis, Redstone, Gold, Ender Pearl, Slimeball, Sticky Resin, Glue, Iron, Wrought Iron , Glass, Bronze, Lava, Water, Soularium\n1 Steam | Bronze | Nether Quartz , Sulfur, Rubber, Red Alloy , Obzinite, Obsidian [ 3 ] , Diamond, Lead [ 4 ] , Bismuth [ 5 ] , Steel | Nether [ 6 ]\n2 LV | 32 | Steel | Oil, Seed Oil, Polyethylene, Light Fuel, Refined Glue, Advanced Glue, Hydrogen, Oxygen, Helium, Nitrogen, Lithium, Sodium, Annealed Copper, Magnalium, Cupronickel, Silver [ 7 ] , Nickel [ 7 ] , Electrum, Glowstone [ 8 ] , Zinc, Cryolite, Chlorine, Damascus Steel, Vanadium, Vanadiumsteel, Molybdenum, Vis Shards, metal blocks | Cupronickel | Twilight Forest\n3 MV | 128 | Aluminium | Redstone Alloy, Cobalt, Ardite, Thaumium, Void Metal, Manasteel, Energetic Alloy, Pulsating Iron, Dark Steel, Electrical Steel, Soularium, Chrome, Silicon Solar Grade , Kanthal, IC2 Coolant, Diesel, Benzene, Stainless Steel | Kanthal\n4 HV | 512 | Stainless Steel | Vibrant Alloy, Eye of Ender [ 9 ] , Neodymium, PTFE, Radium, Radon, Rutile, Shadow Metal, Titanium | Nichrome | Glass (vanilla) | Rocket T1 Pers. Dimension Last Millenium The End [ 6 ]\n5 EV | 2k | Titanium | Tungsten, Tungstensteel, Radon, End Steel, Crystalline Alloy, Melodic Alloy, Enderium, TPV-Alloy, HSS-G, Platinum, Iridium, Terrasteel | TPV-Alloy | Managlass Reinforced Glass TRB Glass Block [ 10 ] | Rocket T2, Spectre\n6 IV | 8k | Tungstensteel | Naquadah, Stellar Alloy, Osmium, HSS-S, Elementium, Gaia Spirit, Rhodium, Palladium | HSS-G HSS-S | Alfglass TsRB Glass Block [ 11 ] Thorium Yttrium Glass Block | Rocket T3\n7 LuV | 32k | Rhodium-Plated Palladium | Europium | Naquadah Naquadah Alloy | RPPRB Glass Block [ 12 ]"

The capstone of this tier is the Mk I. Fusion Reactor; it enables crafting Europium, which in turn gates T5 rockets, europium-doped wafers, the Crystal circuit line, and more."
- candidate chunk-1a9787fd5da78e0eaf6dfc810f0de8a5 doc=doc-45ac5ed5cca700a24048b98b03470923 sections=[Planets > Tier 1] text="Tier 1\nSurface of the Moon\nT1 Moon\nOre Vein | Weight | Frequency | Small Ore | Per Chunk | Fluid Reservoir | Size\nBauxite | 80 | 24.9% | Meteoric Iron | 8 | Helium-3 | 0-425\nChalcopyrite & Copper | 80 | 24.9% | Salt Water | 0-200\nTin & Cassiterite | 50 | 15.6%\nGalena & Silver | 40 | 12.5%\nBastnasite & Monazite | 30 | 9.3%\nQuartzite & Quartz | 20 | 6.2%\nIlmenite | 16 | 5.0%\nWulfenite & Molybdenum | 5 | 1.6%\nOther Notes\nMoon Dungeons spawn randomly underground. Space Villagers spawn randomly around space villages. They have no trades or drops."
- candidate chunk-10174f2d9d907588e988091242077ef2 doc=doc-45ac5ed5cca700a24048b98b03470923 sections=[Planets] text="The Galaxy Map of the Solar System.\nThere are many different planets, asteroid belts, and star systems within GTNH. To reach them, launch a rocket into space and select a destination on the galaxy map. Some destinations (e.g. moons and space stations) only appear when zoomed into the host planet's orbit. Confirming the location then instantly teleports you to the planet at some very high altitude and inside a lander that carries you down to the surface. The rocket and launch pads are retrieved from the lander upon a complete stop, but sometimes it bugs out and never fully stops. If that happens, place a hopper underneath the lander to retrieve the items. Later on, use the Space Elevator to travel to other planets without any rocket fuel.\nThe tier of a planet (visible in NEI ) determines the minimum tier rocket needed to travel there. For example, a T2 rocket or better is required to reach any T2 planet, such as Mars. There are 8 tiers of rockets in total so the only way to reach the last tier of planets (T9) in the Amun-Ra system is with a Mothership .\nEvery planet has its own set of ore veins, small ore veins, and underground fluid reservoirs (visible in NEI ). Some planets also have unique features such as a specific mob that spawns, a Space Dungeon , or even abandoned structures from past civilizations. The following sections lists all relevant information for every planet with certain items highlighted BLUE to signify high importance or value (e.g. a new ore vein). Note that some planets are gas giants which means a space station must be built in their orbit before using the Planetary Gas Siphon to extract their resources.\nImportant: | See here for a list of dimension IDs. See here for a breakdown of each ore vein. See here for Void Miner drop rates."
- candidate chunk-5ff54a6f190364d05242ad8bcac58ed8 doc=doc-0b20024c57c212406bf68b660f2eaf30 sections=[Rockets > Crafting Tier 2+ Rockets] text="Crafting Tier 2+ Rockets\nEntrance to a Moon Dungeon\nThe NASA Workbench requires new schematics to craft higher tiers of rockets. These schematics are found in dungeons on planets in tier order. Tier 2 rocket schematics, for example, can be found in the reward room of Moon Dungeons. Dungeon entrances are deep holes on the surface that have unnatural brick blocks on the bottom.\nThe dungeons are naturally generated and so may be difficult to find. It is important to mark the location of each dungeon found as the coordinates are the same across each planet.\nHigher tiers of rockets require different kinds of Rocket Fuels ."
- candidate chunk-dd32ee9a7437c9bdc478d307b750b39f doc=doc-04ef7ad9e1765bcf95cbd804c813863b sections=[Ultimate Voltage (UV) > Progression > Tier 7 Rocket] text="Tier 7 Rocket\nThe Tier 7 Rocket is available as soon as the player is able to power their Assembly Line with two UV Energy Hatches to overclock it to UHV. It unlocks four new planets: Haumea, Makemake, Pluto, and the Kuiper Belt, which should only be mined using Void Miners at this point. The most useful planet to mine is Haumea, which has the earliest source of Nether Star Ore as well as a high chance for Infused Gold Ore, making it very useful for setting up the second tier of Naquadah Fuel. Other new ores are Borax, Red Garnet, Yellow Garnet, and Chrysotile. The available underground fluids are Hydrofluoric Acid on Makemake, and Oxygen, Nitrogen, Fluorine, and Liquid Air on Pluto.\nThe dungeons containing the next tier of schematic are found on Pluto, alongside the Draconic Core Schematic for the Draconic Evolution Fusion Crafter . It is highly recommended to place the Tier 8 Rocket Schematic in a singleblock Scanner (a Research Station in Scanner mode will not work) and duplicate it using a UV Circuit Assembler, because two Tier 8 Rocket Schematics will be needed: one for making the Tier 8 Rocket, and one to be researched for the Shuttle Schematic for the Mothership ."
