- candidate chunk-996a60daafc37f1bcf77455008b22c82 doc=doc-d8847ae5c01144e82bc225fe54375054 sections=[Zero Point Module (ZPM)] text="Zero Point Voltage (ZPM) is the seventh electric tier in GregTech: New Horizons. This tier is largely centered around building the infrastructure necessary to construct the next tier of Fusion Reactor, which is necessary for progression into UV. In addition, this tier introduces TecTech, the source of many late game multi-blocks. It also sees additional power generation with the availability of the Large Naquadah Reactor and MK2 Fusion Controller, and a new source of resources in the Void Miner . However, similar to LuV, material costs and processing requirements continue to rise and so infrastructure such as a Mega Electric Blast Furnace , Mega Vacuum Freezer , and Mega Chemical Reactor may be necessary. Circuits get a major improvement with the addition of Europium-doped wafers. The PCB Factory is also build-able at this stage, though its quest is UV. ZPM is generally considered to mark the transition from midgame to lategame."
- candidate chunk-6f787dc8fe247b2599f12270525e6285 doc=doc-d38e0fb2c49edead8e172a0081f57d61 sections=[Extreme Voltage (EV)] text="Extreme Voltage (EV) is the fourth electric tier in GregTech: New Horizons. In this tier, several mechanics are introduced such as Applied Energistics 2 (abbreviated as AE2) and the IndustrialCraft 2 Nuclear Reactors . This tier is largely centered around three big goals: starting an AE2 setup, traveling to Mars for new and important resources, and developing centralized electric infrastructure. It introduces several new multiblocks which greatly speed up many processes, but especially ore processing with the introduction of the Industrial Centrifuge , Industrial Maceration Stack , Ore Washing Plant , and others."
- candidate chunk-dd68c611d886bf93d3589f0e90126008 doc=doc-3dbc9b7b97cfc0b8fbe14bc95a57ccc0 sections=[Electricity > Trivia] text="Trivia\n- GregTech's current power system is called the GT Enet API (Enet).\n- Since version 5.0 (for Minecraft 1.7.2), GregTech has its own Energy System since GregoriusT wasn't satisfied with IC2 Experimental's Energy System.\nThe reasons of why I removed compatibility to the IC² Enet are that Cable Loss doesn't work, that the Network doesn't have Packets anymore and that it switched from Integer to Double (what is horrible for larger Energy Storages). Not to mention that it is very hard to have control over Energy flow without constantly registering and unregistering TileEntities. — GregoriusT"
- candidate chunk-05d33b53def3fa0645ccb3b4e9e5ffcb doc=doc-04ef7ad9e1765bcf95cbd804c813863b sections=[Ultimate Voltage (UV)] text="Ultimate Voltage (UV) is the eighth electric tier in GregTech: New Horizons. It is largely centered around the new Wetware circuit line, which introduces new multiblocks like the Quantum Computer and Research Station , as well a-s the new technologies that are unlocked with them, like the third tier of Fusion Reactor . It also introduces many multiblocks for improving infrastructure, including the Mega Alloy Blast Smelter , Component Assembly Line , and Space Elevator , as well as key automation tools like the Stocking Input Hatch."
- candidate chunk-223f6ac44ad47f65c142ca0045b909d0 doc=doc-4f4c543e0ece55aaf03e88c75a851b04 sections=[Extended Mega Ultimate Voltage (UXV)] text="Welcome to Extended Mega Ultimate Voltage (UXV) the thirteenth and final tier in GregTech: New Horizons. This tier begins with the production of Magnetohydrodynamically Constrained Star Matter (MHDCSM) and ends with a Stargate . In UXV, expect to spam even more machines and drastically increase power production to support laser energy hatches."
- candidate chunk-d178e40720f81120beb81ff7d2f5f7ea doc=doc-a509849f031bb1dadb346a8565bf75e8 sections=[Mega Ultimate Voltage (UMV) > Infrastructure > Power > Power Eye of Harmony] text="Power Eye of Harmony\nThe second viable method of endgame power generation is the power-generating Eye of Harmony. After improving an EoH to 7/7/7 or better, it becomes net-positive on power as long as it does not run any overclocks or parallels. Power-generating EoHs can produce a maximum of 12.87T EU/t when running the T10 Deep Dark recipe with 9/9/9 casings, meaning that reaching the Stargate typically requires at least a hundred of them."
- candidate chunk-d76a162f5e2260296cff7570635391d4 doc=doc-0acf46d9067c834f0c19087002ed00b0 sections=[Insane Voltage (IV)] text="Insane Voltage (IV) is the fifth electric tier in GregTech: New Horizons. In this tier, multiple new multiblocks and mechanics are introduced, such as the Assembly Line , the Precise Assembler , and many more. This is also the tier where many parts of magic (Thaumcraft, etc.) are unlocked for players to use; however, as always, watch your warp effects if you have been getting warp. While transitioning from the old tier to a new tier is usually the hard part, the IV tier is by far the hardest tier to get through. If you managed to get through EV tier, prepare yourself because this tier requires a very heavy transition in almost every aspect of your base, including infrastructure and crafting. At this point, manual crafting is no longer a feasible option. With this being said, be prepared for a big change to your base!"
- candidate chunk-8020be5e3374a08380c78b2c3d21be17 doc=doc-626c925b618145e4e43655f7673b36b4 sections=[Low Voltage (LV)] text="Low Voltage (LV) is the first electric tier in GregTech: New Horizons. With access to electricity comes new hazards and mechanics, but also far more variety of specialized machines that can process materials into new parts with greater efficiency."
- candidate chunk-a8f441006abca56356cb8a5f143676d6 doc=doc-3dbc9b7b97cfc0b8fbe14bc95a57ccc0 sections=[Electricity] text="The Energy Unit (EU) is GregTech's unit of electrical energy. EU is used to run GregTech and IC2 machines. Every Tier offers multiple types of Power Generation options at LV and above. EU is also compatible with machines from Applied Energistics 2 and other machines that accept Redstone Flux (RF), converting at a rate of 1 EU to 3.6 RF. The reverse is not possible; RF cannot be converted to EU."
- candidate chunk-3479f7c04a1a2e72217e53b882f388f3 doc=doc-2e161bb7490c4c12a7e195acd1873420 sections=[Insanely Ultimate Voltage (UIV) > Infrastructure > Power > Dyson Swarm] text="Dyson Swarm\nThe Dyson Swarm Ground Unit\nThe Dyson Swarm Ground Unit (Dyson) is a new power generation multiblock that is unlocked with UMV circuits. It can produce 337B EU/t when placed on the planet Horus, which is the only place Dysons should be built, at the cost of 3.6 ML of Gelid Cryotheum and 64 Dyson Swarm Modules every hour, as well as one or more Quantum Computers making at least 100,000 Computation. In order to transport the power back from Horus, a Lapotronic Supercapacitor should be used to send it into the wireless network; this can be replaced with an Energized Wireless Dynamo Hatch in UMV."

- candidate chunk-4ebba377064a29e9d4a2afac5f24e1a7 doc=doc-d8847ae5c01144e82bc225fe54375054 sections=[Zero Point Module (ZPM) > Infrastructure Improvements > Power Generation] text="Power Generation\nIn ZPM, most existing power generation methods from earlier tiers, such as Nitrobenzene , Rocket Fuel, and Nuclear Reactors , remain viable, but start to fall off due to increasing power demands and a lack of upgrades. One notable upgrade is Huge Turbines for all gas, steam, or plasma-based power, which are unlocked with Americium.\nPlasma, on the other hand, is still viable and will continue to be viable for many more tiers. In addition to the aforementioned Huge Turbines, plasma benefits from better fuels in the form of Tin Plasma or Runite Plasma (although the latter requires a UV Alloy Blast Smelter ), better production rates using the Mk2 Fusion Reactor, and the XL Plasma Turbine and XL Supercritical Steam Turbine to greatly improve power throughput.\nA Large Naquadah Reactor.\nZPM also unlocks one of the strongest power generation methods in the mid and late game: Naquadah Fuel, often abbreviated to Naqfuel. Power is produced by inputting Naqfuel into a Large Naquadah Reactor (LNR), alongside Liquid Air, a coolant (Gelid Cryotheum), and an excited liquid (Molten Atomic Separation Catalyst), all of which will need to be produced in large quantities by players seeking to use the fuel for power. Two tiers of Naqfuel are available in ZPM: tier 1 requires the Mk2 Fusion Reactor, while tier 2 is produced by combining tier 1 fuel with a couple other ingredients in an overclocked Mega Chemical Reactor . Each LNR can generate up to 42.9M EU/t with Naqfuel 1, and up to 101.2M EU/t with Naqfuel 2."
- candidate chunk-545958da3be50add2bef43de062e3efc doc=doc-d38e0fb2c49edead8e172a0081f57d61 sections=[Extreme Voltage (EV) > Progression > Energy crisis - part 1] text="Energy crisis - part 1\nYou might've found you are struggling to power up those EV single block machines with just several HV generators (either automated or not). If your main energy production is from oil, it's recommended to build a multiblock combustion engine and upgrade to cetane-boosted diesel if you didn't in HV. It costs about 4 stacks of titanium to make and without boosting with oxygen, produces 2048EU/t at 100% efficiency, and if boosted with oxygen, produces 6144EU/t at 150% efficiency. Because of the boosting mechanism, it's recommended to keep it boosting. It also requires a small amount of lubricant to run.\nAnother option is large gas turbine. It runs at efficiency based on the turbine you put into the controller. The gas input must be close to the optimal flow rate the turbine describes, or efficiency will drop. There is also a replacement, the oxidation fuel unit, where it's the same but does not need a turbine but needs oxygen to run but produces steam, but it's not recommended because it only produces 2048EU/t while costing more than the gas turbine. Also, its efficiency caps at 100% while large gas turbines are based on the turbine and can easily go beyond 100% efficiency.\nIf you want to make platline or got some platinum, you can build your first nuclear generator. You can check the Nuclear Reactor page to find what design that fits best for you. I recommend you to use the 1280EU/t design as it only needs 4 chambers (3 chambers for the main block). Build as many as you need as the reactor is pretty cheap in both maintaining and its build cost."
- candidate chunk-281335ed0efda71a27ab652405366a1b doc=doc-04ef7ad9e1765bcf95cbd804c813863b sections=[Ultimate Voltage (UV) > Infrastructure Improvements > Power] text="Power\nUV is the point at which power generation from the early game falls off: Nitrobenzene, Rocket Fuel, and Nuclear Reactors can last up to this point, but should be replaced by the start of the next tier. Plasma or Naquadah Fuel, both of which are viable in UV and able to scale for multiple more tiers, should be used instead. Plasma gains access to the Fusion Reactor Mk3, which provides both access to the best plasma chain fuels and an overclock on earlier fusion recipes to reduce the need for spam, and the Stocking Input Hatch helps parallelize on slower fusion steps. It also benefits from new better turbine materials, such as Cosmic Neutronium. Naquadah Fuel also benefits from these improvements for its slow fusion step, and gains access to Haumea Void Miners , the Space Elevator, and the Mega Apiary to help source its inputs."
- candidate chunk-d178e40720f81120beb81ff7d2f5f7ea doc=doc-a509849f031bb1dadb346a8565bf75e8 sections=[Mega Ultimate Voltage (UMV) > Infrastructure > Power > Power Eye of Harmony] text="Power Eye of Harmony\nThe second viable method of endgame power generation is the power-generating Eye of Harmony. After improving an EoH to 7/7/7 or better, it becomes net-positive on power as long as it does not run any overclocks or parallels. Power-generating EoHs can produce a maximum of 12.87T EU/t when running the T10 Deep Dark recipe with 9/9/9 casings, meaning that reaching the Stargate typically requires at least a hundred of them."
- candidate chunk-4f552df6ef7c8df800ed6524c02be6c5 doc=doc-a509849f031bb1dadb346a8565bf75e8 sections=[Mega Ultimate Voltage (UMV) > Infrastructure > Power] text="Power\nThe Eye of Harmony is extremely power-hungry, and will likely consume most of the player's power generation while they progress through its tiers. Without any overclocks its power usage is fairly modest, on the order of 100B EU/t, but it is also extremely slow, and adding even a single overclock increases it to multiple trillion EU/t due to its very inefficient overclocks. In UMV, there are three viable sources of power to run the Eye of Harmony and its supporting infrastructure."
- candidate chunk-c56c4b548e2908843346415fe4d84217 doc=doc-709fa05c9dc5e6bdd1e4979266413ad2 sections=[Power Generation > Enriched Naquadah/Naquadria Reactors] text="Enriched Naquadah/Naquadria Reactors\nSee More: Large Naquadah Reactor and Naquadah Fuel Refinery\nSingleblock naquadah reactors produce large amounts of power at a constant eu/t, as stated on the machine's tooltip. These drain power depending by depleting tiberium/enriched naquadah/naquadria screw/rods/long rods into normal naquadah screws/rods/long rods, converting it into an internal buffer that is drained at a constant eu/t. The efficiency of the machine multiplies the EU potential of the rod, giving the true energy per item. They are best for remote mining drills and should not be used as a main source of power.\nThe Large Naquadah Reactor (LNR) is much different from the singleblock reactors. Instead of rods, it consumes nuclear based fuels for breeding and naquadah based fuels for power generation. There are six tiers of naquadah fuel: MK1 and MK2 fuels are made in a fusion reactor while MK3 and above are made in the Naquadah Fuel Refinery , a multiblock available late into UHV. The LNR requires liquid air to function and optionally can be supplied with o ne type of coolant and one type of booster to further improve power output. Coolants increase the efficiency of the naquadah based fuels (IC2 coolant: 105% efficiency, super coolant: 150% efficiency, Cryotheum: 275%, and Tachyon Rich Temporal Fluid: 500%). Boosters increase both the fuel consumption and output of the reactor (molten caesium: 2x, molten U-235: 3x, molten naquadah: 4x, molten atomic separation catalyst: 16x, and spatially enlarged fluid: 64x).\nThe LNR is much better than plasma turbines because there is no turbine durability to worry about, higher power output, and less space required."
- candidate chunk-4c121b8e98d3b7c3f3bb0aa886875c70 doc=doc-c9afb2e028c318ddc2f228663f5523fe sections=[Extremely Ultimate Voltage (UEV) > Infrastructure Improvements > Power > Generation] text="Generation\nNaquadah Fuel and Plasma remain the only viable power sources in UEV. Naqfuel can be upgraded to Mk4, or even Mk5, by using Tier 2 coils in the Naquadah Fuel Refinery and combining Mk3 fuel with various materials. Prior to the DTPF, only the weaker recipes for both fuels are viable, but the DTPF can be used to produce Hypogen and Infinity fast enough to use the stronger recipes, improving the efficiency of Mk3 fuel. Plasma gains access to the Compact 4 to increase fuel production, but Infinity remains the best available rotor material."
- candidate chunk-5e57a74464432107837c03099bfbc7b6 doc=doc-3dbc9b7b97cfc0b8fbe14bc95a57ccc0 sections=[Electricity > Overclocking] text="Overclocking\nMain Article: Overclocking\nMachines of higher tiers can receive overclocks to speed up their recipes.\nRegular overclocks double the recipe's speed and total EU consumption, leading to quadrupled EU/t usage. For this reason, overclocking is also called 2/4 overclocking.\nA perfect overclock instead quadruples the recipe's speed while keeping the total EU consumption the same. This still leads to quadrupled EU/t usage. For this reason, perfect overclocking is also called 4/4 overclocking.\nMachines typically receive one overclock per voltage tier above the minimum required for a recipe.\nSingleblock machines can only ever regular overclock.\nMultiblock machines perfect overclock on a case-by-case basis; their tooltips contain information on how to achieve perfect overclocks or upgrade overclocks to perfect overclocks. If no information is mentioned, they will regular overclock.\nMultiblocks with two energy hatches of the same voltage will always draw 4A to overclock a recipe to a tier higher than the energy hatch voltage (since 4A of the lower voltage contains the same EU as 1A of the higher voltage). This also allows them to run recipes with a minimum requirement of that higher voltage tier."
- candidate chunk-33506a46db8cb160a686236470dc2d56 doc=doc-626c925b618145e4e43655f7673b36b4 sections=[Low Voltage (LV) > Energy Loss] text="Energy Loss\nThe tooltip for GT cables show their max voltage, max Amperage and Loss per Meter/Ampere. A cable or wire will catch fire if it receives more than its max voltage or max amperage. A normal 1x Tin Cable will lose 1 volt for each block (meter) length of the cable. Uncoated wires lose even more per block and present a shock hazard, so never use bare wire to connect machines. If the distance between the power input and machine is 10 blocks long, and the generator is generating 32 volts in 1 amp, then there will only be 22 volts in 1 amp remaining on the other end of the cable. With a LV battery buffer with 4 LV batteries inside, it would send 32 volts in up to 4 amps, but after 10 blocks there will only be 22 volts each amp remaining. Lossless cables are gated behind Redstone Alloy and the Electric Blast Furnace , so until available players are well advised to keep cables as short as possible."
- candidate chunk-31999df98437a688bc59ee797433641e doc=doc-8666c624815c422fd88b0cdfb5cbfb2a sections=[High Voltage (HV) > Progression > First Energy Crisis] text="First Energy Crisis\nYou may find yourself struggling to power HV EBFs and possibly even your singleblock machines for long periods of time, as each HV machine you make consumes 4x (not accounting for efficiency loss) the amount of fuel compared to your MV generators. You should expand and fully automate your energy production setup.\nIn HV, combustion generators gain a massive buff thanks to the addition of Cetane-Boosted Diesel, which boasts a whopping 1M EU per cell, a massive expansion over Diesel. In addition, Benzene is heavily improved thanks to the Distillation Tower and Pyrolyse Oven coil upgrades which significantly speed up Benzene production. The Distillation Tower will even produce Phenol and Toluene which can be burned in Gas Turbines if you're in a pinch. Note that these infrastructure upgrades require significant amounts of resources upfront, but the infrastructure investment easily carries over into EV."

Power is produced by inputting Naqfuel into a Large Naquadah Reactor (LNR), alongside Liquid Air, a coolant (Gelid Cryotheum), and an excited liquid (Molten Atomic Separation Catalyst), all of which will need to be produced in large quantities by players seeking to use the fuel for power. ## Turn 6 — How do battery buffers draw and output amps?

Expected goal rubric: Explain battery-buffer input and output amperage behavior.
Expected constraint rubrics: [Current stage is MV.]
Corpus sufficient: true

### Lane 1 — memory=false

Settings: `{Mode:grounded Strategy:structural TaskMemory:false Rewrite:false}`
Generation: `{Provider:deepseek Endpoint:https://api.deepseek.com/chat/completions MainModel:deepseek-flash AuxModel:deepseek-flash Temperature:0 MaxTokens:2048}`
Retrieval: `{CandidateK:10 ContextK:3 Threshold:0.6 Calibrated:true Calibration:week-05/calibration.json}`
Answer status: answer
Refusal: false
Answer: Battery Buffers draw and output amps based on the number of batteries inserted: each battery added increases the buffer's capacity by +2 amps input and +1 amp output. The buffer itself does not store power; batteries matching the tier must be inserted for it to function. Because the amperage output varies with the number of inserted batteries, the cable must have sufficient capacity to handle the buffer's maximum output, which matters more when multiple machines request amps simultaneously over a longer cable. Battery Buffers also store EU Energy at a 5% loss cost and can be made in variable capacities between 1x and 16x internal slots.

- candidate chunk-268950b23008748d06a2400f6acdb574 doc=doc-3dbc9b7b97cfc0b8fbe14bc95a57ccc0 sections=[Electricity > Voltage and Amperage > Amperage] text="Amperage\nIn code, amps are pushed, not pulled. However, they are only pushed when requested by machines. Thus, for all scopes except pack development, you can assume amps are only ever pulled as needed.\nMachines and energy hatches draw amps to fill their internal EU buffers. The capacity of the internal buffer is listed as \"Capacity:\" in the machine's or hatch's tooltip. When processing a recipe, a machine will draw from its internal buffer. The current contents of the internal buffer can be checked with the Portable Scanner .\nThe maximum amount of amps a machine can draw is either listed on its tooltip or recipe-dependent. A machine can draw up to a maximum of ⌊ 2 × Recipe Usage EU/t Voltage Tier EU Limit ⌋ + 1 {\\displaystyle {\\displaystyle \\left\\lfloor {\\frac {2\\times {\\text{Recipe Usage EU/t}}}{\\text{Voltage Tier EU Limit}}}\\right\\rfloor +1}} amps to fill its internal buffer. This means that if the machine isn't processing a recipe, it will draw 1 amp to refill the internal buffer. Recipe amperages listed in NEI are a recommended number to fill the internal buffer at a rate that breaks even against usage.\nSeveral common amperages of GregTech machines can be found in the table below. \"Drawing\" means \"draws up to\", unless otherwise stated.\nOutput amps contain the maximum EU for the outputting machine's voltage tier.\nMachine | Notes\nMachine: Singleblock Generators | Notes: Output 1A of its tier\nMachine: Energy Hatches | Notes: Draws 2A (second amp mostly for extra draw for loss)\nMachine: Step-up Transformers | Notes: Draws 4A lower voltage, outputs 1A higher voltage\nMachine: Step-down Transformers | Notes: Draws 1A higher voltage, outputs 4A lower voltage\nMachine: Battery Buffers | Notes: Draws 2A per Battery, outputs 1A per Battery.\nMachine: Battery Chargers | Notes: Draws 4-8A per Battery, outputs 2-4A per Battery correspondingly.\nMachine: Arc Furnace | Notes: Most recipes require 3A to break even against EU/t usage\nMachine: Thermal Centrifuge | Notes: Most recipes require 2A to break even against EU/t usage"
- candidate chunk-7377a2351dcc2bc55102f8f2bc69e9e2 doc=doc-626c925b618145e4e43655f7673b36b4 sections=[Low Voltage (LV) > EU (Energy Units) Mechanics] text="EU (Energy Units) Mechanics\nGregTech energy consists of Voltage and Amperage. A Low Voltage (LV) GT generator will output 32 volts (32V) in 1 amperage (1A/1 amp). Four generators on the same cable will output 32 volts in 4 amps combined. 32 volts in 4 amps is not the same thing as MV (Medium Voltage - 128 volts per amp). This can be compared to four cables containing 32 volts each, or one 4x Cable. The x rating of a cable (x1, x2, x4, etc.) denotes its maximum amperage. The general rule of thumb is your cables should have an equal Amp capacity to the number of generators/batteries attached. Battery Buffers output 1A per battery , while virtually every generator puts out 1A. Having a cable with enough Amperage capacity for all of them at once prevents fires and explosions .\nFor more advanced and complete information, see Electricity ."
- candidate chunk-aa8ebc6db27b8cebbe7f671371379b65 doc=doc-626c925b618145e4e43655f7673b36b4 sections=[Low Voltage (LV) > Battery Buffers] text="Battery Buffers\nBattery Buffers are used to store EU Energy, at a 5% loss cost. Battery Buffers can be made in variable capacities between 1x and 16x internal slots. The Buffer itself does not store power; batteries matching the tier have to be inserted into the Battery Buffer to make it function. Each battery added will increase the buffer's capacity by +2 amps input and +1 amp output. Since buffers can have a variable amperage output based on the number of inserted batteries, care must be taken to ensure the cable has sufficient capacity to handle the buffer's maximum output. This becomes more relevant when multiple machines are all requesting amps at the same time over a longer cable."
- candidate chunk-137405157af87dd0f3eee24e6971f51d doc=doc-626c925b618145e4e43655f7673b36b4 sections=[Low Voltage (LV) > GT Electric Machines] text="GT Electric Machines\nMachine GUI. Auto-output buttons circled in yellow.\nLV Electric machines have some new features that were not available with Steam-age machinery. For example, the steam exhaust is replaced with an automatic output. This output's facing can be changed with a wrench similar to how the exhaust was configured on Steam machines. Just like their Steam counterparts, all electric GT machines cannot be input or output from their front face. To enable automatic output, open the GUI of the machine and click on the button at the lower left corner. There are two automatic output toggle buttons; the left one is for liquid and the right one is for items.\nElectrolyzer clay dust recipe. The MV power requirement is circled in yellow.\nGregTech machinery is divided by tier, which corresponds to a specific voltage - LV (32 V), MV (128 V), HV (512 V) etc. Most recipes are gated to a specific voltage/tier or higher, which is shown with the recipe in NEI (Not Enough Items). If the recipe requires more than one amp it will also be listed here. Pay careful attention to what tier a recipe requires - with LV machinery, MV and higher recipes are inaccessible.\nFor example, putting Clay Dust through the Electrolyzer to get Aluminium dust requires 120 EU/t (MV). Since the Basic Electrolyzer is only able to do 32EU/t, this recipe is unavailable until the Advanced Electrolyzer (MV).\nRecipes close to the voltage limit for LV (32 EU/t) may cause the machine to request multiple amps, especially if there is any cable loss. A single LV generator will often not be capable of supplying enough power if the machine is placed too far away. Most GT machines will accept up to 2 amps. A second generator or Battery Buffer may be needed to keep a a high cost recipe running. If a machine runs out of power, it will stall resetting any progress but not losing the materials. A soft mallet can be used to temporarily disable a machine to give a buffer time to recharge or wait until more power generation is available.\nSome machines require multiple amps to run at all, such as the Arc Furnace and Electric Blast Furnace . The Arc Furnace requires 3 amps to function and can accept up to 7 amps. Checking any recipe for the Arc Furnace will show Usage XX/t, voltage 32 EU/t"
- candidate chunk-05d33b53def3fa0645ccb3b4e9e5ffcb doc=doc-04ef7ad9e1765bcf95cbd804c813863b sections=[Ultimate Voltage (UV)] text="Ultimate Voltage (UV) is the eighth electric tier in GregTech: New Horizons. It is largely centered around the new Wetware circuit line, which introduces new multiblocks like the Quantum Computer and Research Station , as well a-s the new technologies that are unlocked with them, like the third tier of Fusion Reactor . It also introduces many multiblocks for improving infrastructure, including the Mega Alloy Blast Smelter , Component Assembly Line , and Space Elevator , as well as key automation tools like the Stocking Input Hatch."
- candidate chunk-33506a46db8cb160a686236470dc2d56 doc=doc-626c925b618145e4e43655f7673b36b4 sections=[Low Voltage (LV) > Energy Loss] text="Energy Loss\nThe tooltip for GT cables show their max voltage, max Amperage and Loss per Meter/Ampere. A cable or wire will catch fire if it receives more than its max voltage or max amperage. A normal 1x Tin Cable will lose 1 volt for each block (meter) length of the cable. Uncoated wires lose even more per block and present a shock hazard, so never use bare wire to connect machines. If the distance between the power input and machine is 10 blocks long, and the generator is generating 32 volts in 1 amp, then there will only be 22 volts in 1 amp remaining on the other end of the cable. With a LV battery buffer with 4 LV batteries inside, it would send 32 volts in up to 4 amps, but after 10 blocks there will only be 22 volts each amp remaining. Lossless cables are gated behind Redstone Alloy and the Electric Blast Furnace , so until available players are well advised to keep cables as short as possible."
- candidate chunk-fdf371b1592b7ed0250bf63b741b8cb9 doc=doc-3dbc9b7b97cfc0b8fbe14bc95a57ccc0 sections=[Electricity > Cables and Loss] text="Cables and Loss\nGregTech EU can be transferred using GregTech Cables.\nRather than priority by destination, power priority is given by cardinal direction. Cables will push power in the following priority:\n- Down\n- Up\n- North\n- South\n- West\n- East\nWhen producing power, each generator will always attempt to empty all of its internal buffer at once. This can cause one generator in a cable network to use fuel more rapidly than another, with order depending on tile entity processing and changing on chunk reloads.\nAll GT Cables have a max Voltage, max Amperage and a Loss/Meter/Ampere, indicated on their tooltip:\n- Cables which receive amps containing more EU than their maximum Voltage will catch fire and melt.\n- Cables that have more amps travelling through them than their maximum Amperage limit will catch fire and melt. Note that machines can request more Amperage than strictly required by their active recipe.\n- Each energy packet travelling through a cable loses Voltage per block travelled. The amount of EU lost is indicated by the Loss/Meter/Ampere number.\nEach Material has 1x, 2x, 4x, 8x, 12x, and 16x uninsulated Wires. Most of these have cable variants, except for the superconductor wires, as well as graphene, Ichorium, hypogen, Black Plutonium, Awakened Draconium, Infinity, and SpaceTime wires. Note that Uninsulated Wires have twice the Loss/Meter/Ampere as Insulated Cables. The only exceptions to this are Red alloy and Redstone alloy (as their cables are lossless, while the wires are not), Quantium, Lumiium, and Signalium (four times the loss when uninsulated), and Bedrockium (8x loss when uninsulated).\nFor example:\n- A 1x Tin Cable can handle 1A containing 32EU at a loss of 1 EU-Volt/Meter/Ampere. This means that 1 amp can travel 32 blocks before it dies.\n- A 1x Tin Wire can handle 1A containing 32EU at a loss of 2 EU-Volt/Meter/Ampere. In this case, the amp can travel 16 blocks only.\nBelow is a table of the current properties of various types of cables in GregTech: Note: In 2.8.0, many cables received maximum amperage and loss changes. This table is accurate as of version 2.8.1+.\nMaterial | Max Voltage [ 1 ] | Amp [ 2 ] | Loss [ 3 ] | Decay [ 4 ]\nMaterial: Tin | Max Voltage [ 1 ]: LV (32) | Amp [ 2 ]: 1 | Loss [ 3 ]: 1 | Decay ["
- candidate chunk-8020be5e3374a08380c78b2c3d21be17 doc=doc-626c925b618145e4e43655f7673b36b4 sections=[Low Voltage (LV)] text="Low Voltage (LV) is the first electric tier in GregTech: New Horizons. With access to electricity comes new hazards and mechanics, but also far more variety of specialized machines that can process materials into new parts with greater efficiency."
- candidate chunk-fd0e7d7c4b95cf731b2074dbbab5e234 doc=doc-16afb764b518d80604353589d360da21 sections=[Medium Voltage (MV)] text="Medium Voltage (MV) is the second electric tier in GregTech: New Horizons. It introduces fewer new mechanics, but instead improves and expands what the player has already seen in LV ."
- candidate chunk-6f787dc8fe247b2599f12270525e6285 doc=doc-d38e0fb2c49edead8e172a0081f57d61 sections=[Extreme Voltage (EV)] text="Extreme Voltage (EV) is the fourth electric tier in GregTech: New Horizons. In this tier, several mechanics are introduced such as Applied Energistics 2 (abbreviated as AE2) and the IndustrialCraft 2 Nuclear Reactors . This tier is largely centered around three big goals: starting an AE2 setup, traveling to Mars for new and important resources, and developing centralized electric infrastructure. It introduces several new multiblocks which greatly speed up many processes, but especially ore processing with the introduction of the Industrial Centrifuge , Industrial Maceration Stack , Ore Washing Plant , and others."
- evidence chunk-268950b23008748d06a2400f6acdb574 doc=doc-3dbc9b7b97cfc0b8fbe14bc95a57ccc0 sections=[Electricity > Voltage and Amperage > Amperage] text="Amperage\nIn code, amps are pushed, not pulled. However, they are only pushed when requested by machines. Thus, for all scopes except pack development, you can assume amps are only ever pulled as needed.\nMachines and energy hatches draw amps to fill their internal EU buffers. The capacity of the internal buffer is listed as \"Capacity:\" in the machine's or hatch's tooltip. When processing a recipe, a machine will draw from its internal buffer. The current contents of the internal buffer can be checked with the Portable Scanner .\nThe maximum amount of amps a machine can draw is either listed on its tooltip or recipe-dependent. A machine can draw up to a maximum of ⌊ 2 × Recipe Usage EU/t Voltage Tier EU Limit ⌋ + 1 {\\displaystyle {\\displaystyle \\left\\lfloor {\\frac {2\\times {\\text{Recipe Usage EU/t}}}{\\text{Voltage Tier EU Limit}}}\\right\\rfloor +1}} amps to fill its internal buffer. This means that if the machine isn't processing a recipe, it will draw 1 amp to refill the internal buffer. Recipe amperages listed in NEI are a recommended number to fill the internal buffer at a rate that breaks even against usage.\nSeveral common amperages of GregTech machines can be found in the table below. \"Drawing\" means \"draws up to\", unless otherwise stated.\nOutput amps contain the maximum EU for the outputting machine's voltage tier.\nMachine | Notes\nMachine: Singleblock Generators | Notes: Output 1A of its tier\nMachine: Energy Hatches | Notes: Draws 2A (second amp mostly for extra draw for loss)\nMachine: Step-up Transformers | Notes: Draws 4A lower voltage, outputs 1A higher voltage\nMachine: Step-down Transformers | Notes: Draws 1A higher voltage, outputs 4A lower voltage\nMachine: Battery Buffers | Notes: Draws 2A per Battery, outputs 1A per Battery.\nMachine: Battery Chargers | Notes: Draws 4-8A per Battery, outputs 2-4A per Battery correspondingly.\nMachine: Arc Furnace | Notes: Most recipes require 3A to break even against EU/t usage\nMachine: Thermal Centrifuge | Notes: Most recipes require 2A to break even against EU/t usage"

### Lane 2 — memory=true

Settings: `{Mode:grounded Strategy:structural TaskMemory:true Rewrite:false}`
Generation: `{Provider:deepseek Endpoint:https://api.deepseek.com/chat/completions MainModel:deepseek-flash AuxModel:deepseek-flash Temperature:0 MaxTokens:2048}`
Retrieval: `{CandidateK:10 ContextK:3 Threshold:0.6 Calibrated:true Calibration:week-05/calibration.json}`
Answer status: answer
Refusal: false
Answer: Battery buffers draw and output amps based on how many batteries are inserted. Each battery added increases the buffer's capacity by +2 amps input and +1 amp output. Equivalently, a battery buffer draws 2A per battery and outputs 1A per battery. Because the output amperage is variable depending on the number of inserted batteries, you must ensure the cable has sufficient capacity to handle the buffer's maximum output, especially when multiple machines request amps at the same time over a longer cable.
Goal state: "Explain how battery buffers draw and output amps in GT:NH" (quote "How do battery buffers draw and output amps?")
Clarifications: `[{Key:current_tier Value:MV Quote:Correction: I have reached MV.} {Key:current_power Value:steam power Quote:I currently have steam power.}]`

- candidate chunk-542935792c1f0cfaa3b3c690a0233a39 doc=doc-3dbc9b7b97cfc0b8fbe14bc95a57ccc0 sections=[Electricity > Output Loss] text="Output Loss\nAny GT Block outputting EU has an energy loss on output. This means there is no such thing as lossless power transfer. A power outputting singleblock will take 8 × 4 t i e r + 2 m a x ( 0 , t i e r − 1 ) {\\displaystyle {\\displaystyle 8\\times 4^{tier}+2^{max(0,tier-1)}}} EU from its internal buffer and output 8 × 4 t i e r {\\displaystyle {\\displaystyle 8\\times 4^{tier}}} EU (which is always the maximum EU one amp of that voltage tier can hold). For example, a LV Combustion Generator draws 33 EU from its internal buffer to generate 1 amp holding 32 EU. The energy lost is therefore 2 m a x ( 0 , t i e r − 1 ) {\\displaystyle {\\displaystyle 2^{max(0,tier-1)}}} .\nTier | Output (EU) | Loss (EU) | Loss (%) | Internal Buffer Consumption (EU)\nTier: ULV | Output (EU): 8 | Loss (EU): 1 | Loss (%): 12.5 | Internal Buffer Consumption (EU): 9\nTier: LV | Output (EU): 32 | Loss (EU): 1 | Loss (%): 3.0303 | Internal Buffer Consumption (EU): 33\nTier: MV | Output (EU): 128 | Loss (EU): 2 | Loss (%): 1.5384 | Internal Buffer Consumption (EU): 130\nTier: HV | Output (EU): 512 | Loss (EU): 4 | Loss (%): 0.77519 | Internal Buffer Consumption (EU): 516\nTier: EV | Output (EU): 2048 | Loss (EU): 8 | Loss (%): 0.38911 | Internal Buffer Consumption (EU): 2056\nTier: IV | Output (EU): 8192 | Loss (EU): 16 | Loss (%): 0.19493 | Internal Buffer Consumption (EU): 8208\nTier: LuV | Output (EU): 32768 | Loss (EU): 32 | Loss (%): 0.097561 | Internal Buffer Consumption (EU): 32800\nTier: ZPM | Output (EU): 131072 | Loss (EU): 64 | Loss (%): 0.048804 | Internal Buffer Consumption (EU): 131136\nTier: UV | Output (EU): 524288 | Loss (EU): 128 | Loss (%): 0.024408 | Internal Buffer Consumption (EU): 524416"
- candidate chunk-df7fb1d7f5beb071b0f8ad99ef260a9b doc=doc-3dbc9b7b97cfc0b8fbe14bc95a57ccc0 sections=[Electricity > Transformers] text="Transformers\nPicture of a Transformer in default mode transferring 1 amp of HV into 4 amps of MV.\nTransformers convert EU between voltage tiers. In Step Up mode, they draw up to 4 amps of a lower voltage to fill their internal buffers, and draw from their internal buffers to produce 1A of a higher voltage. This can allow lower tier amps to power higher tier machines which may only draw 1-2 A for a recipe. Similarly, in Step Down mode, they draw 1 amp of a higher voltage to fill their internal buffers, and draw from their internal buffers to produce up to 4A of a lower voltage. This allows use of higher tier power generators with lower tier machines while preventing explosions. Use a Soft Mallet to switch the mode.\nThe big dot with a plus is the high voltage side and is the front face, while the five smaller circles are the lower voltage sides. Regardless of mode, the big dot is always for the higher voltage. That means in Step Up mode, transformers have one output, five inputs, and in Step Down mode, they have five outputs, one input.\nThe output loss section above applies to transformer outputs as well."
- candidate chunk-0325cb24482f3d9145893abfffa1f0b7 doc=doc-626c925b618145e4e43655f7673b36b4 sections=[Low Voltage (LV) > GT Electric Machines] text="progress but not losing the materials. The Arc Furnace requires 3 amps to function and can accept up to 7 amps. Checking any recipe for the Arc Furnace will show Usage XX/t, voltage 32 EU/t and amperage X. The Electric Blast Furnace is even more demanding, taking four amps of LV minimum for its most basic recipes."
- candidate chunk-ec32d0d4336f8eb8aacc104a896ce2cc doc=doc-3dbc9b7b97cfc0b8fbe14bc95a57ccc0 sections=[Electricity > Multi-Amp Generation] text="Multi-Amp Generation\nMultiblock Generators use Dynamo Hatches to output power. These come in 1A, 4A, 16A, and 64A variants. These generators will always attempt to fill existing generated amps with EU before generating more amps."
- candidate chunk-fdf371b1592b7ed0250bf63b741b8cb9 doc=doc-3dbc9b7b97cfc0b8fbe14bc95a57ccc0 sections=[Electricity > Cables and Loss] text="Cables and Loss\nGregTech EU can be transferred using GregTech Cables.\nRather than priority by destination, power priority is given by cardinal direction. Cables will push power in the following priority:\n- Down\n- Up\n- North\n- South\n- West\n- East\nWhen producing power, each generator will always attempt to empty all of its internal buffer at once. This can cause one generator in a cable network to use fuel more rapidly than another, with order depending on tile entity processing and changing on chunk reloads.\nAll GT Cables have a max Voltage, max Amperage and a Loss/Meter/Ampere, indicated on their tooltip:\n- Cables which receive amps containing more EU than their maximum Voltage will catch fire and melt.\n- Cables that have more amps travelling through them than their maximum Amperage limit will catch fire and melt. Note that machines can request more Amperage than strictly required by their active recipe.\n- Each energy packet travelling through a cable loses Voltage per block travelled. The amount of EU lost is indicated by the Loss/Meter/Ampere number.\nEach Material has 1x, 2x, 4x, 8x, 12x, and 16x uninsulated Wires. Most of these have cable variants, except for the superconductor wires, as well as graphene, Ichorium, hypogen, Black Plutonium, Awakened Draconium, Infinity, and SpaceTime wires. Note that Uninsulated Wires have twice the Loss/Meter/Ampere as Insulated Cables. The only exceptions to this are Red alloy and Redstone alloy (as their cables are lossless, while the wires are not), Quantium, Lumiium, and Signalium (four times the loss when uninsulated), and Bedrockium (8x loss when uninsulated).\nFor example:\n- A 1x Tin Cable can handle 1A containing 32EU at a loss of 1 EU-Volt/Meter/Ampere. This means that 1 amp can travel 32 blocks before it dies.\n- A 1x Tin Wire can handle 1A containing 32EU at a loss of 2 EU-Volt/Meter/Ampere. In this case, the amp can travel 16 blocks only.\nBelow is a table of the current properties of various types of cables in GregTech: Note: In 2.8.0, many cables received maximum amperage and loss changes. This table is accurate as of version 2.8.1+.\nMaterial | Max Voltage [ 1 ] | Amp [ 2 ] | Loss [ 3 ] | Decay [ 4 ]\nMaterial: Tin | Max Voltage [ 1 ]: LV (32) | Amp [ 2 ]: 1 | Loss [ 3 ]: 1 | Decay ["
- evidence chunk-aa8ebc6db27b8cebbe7f671371379b65 doc=doc-626c925b618145e4e43655f7673b36b4 sections=[Low Voltage (LV) > Battery Buffers] text="Battery Buffers\nBattery Buffers are used to store EU Energy, at a 5% loss cost. Battery Buffers can be made in variable capacities between 1x and 16x internal slots. The Buffer itself does not store power; batteries matching the tier have to be inserted into the Battery Buffer to make it function. Each battery added will increase the buffer's capacity by +2 amps input and +1 amp output. Since buffers can have a variable amperage output based on the number of inserted batteries, care must be taken to ensure the cable has sufficient capacity to handle the buffer's maximum output. This becomes more relevant when multiple machines are all requesting amps at the same time over a longer cable."
- evidence chunk-268950b23008748d06a2400f6acdb574 doc=doc-3dbc9b7b97cfc0b8fbe14bc95a57ccc0 sections=[Electricity > Voltage and Amperage > Amperage] text="Amperage\nIn code, amps are pushed, not pulled. However, they are only pushed when requested by machines. Thus, for all scopes except pack development, you can assume amps are only ever pulled as needed.\nMachines and energy hatches draw amps to fill their internal EU buffers. The capacity of the internal buffer is listed as \"Capacity:\" in the machine's or hatch's tooltip. When processing a recipe, a machine will draw from its internal buffer. The current contents of the internal buffer can be checked with the Portable Scanner .\nThe maximum amount of amps a machine can draw is either listed on its tooltip or recipe-dependent. A machine can draw up to a maximum of ⌊ 2 × Recipe Usage EU/t Voltage Tier EU Limit ⌋ + 1 {\\displaystyle {\\displaystyle \\left\\lfloor {\\frac {2\\times {\\text{Recipe Usage EU/t}}}{\\text{Voltage Tier EU Limit}}}\\right\\rfloor +1}} amps to fill its internal buffer. This means that if the machine isn't processing a recipe, it will draw 1 amp to refill the internal buffer. Recipe amperages listed in NEI are a recommended number to fill the internal buffer at a rate that breaks even against usage.\nSeveral common amperages of GregTech machines can be found in the table below. \"Drawing\" means \"draws up to\", unless otherwise stated.\nOutput amps contain the maximum EU for the outputting machine's voltage tier.\nMachine | Notes\nMachine: Singleblock Generators | Notes: Output 1A of its tier\nMachine: Energy Hatches | Notes: Draws 2A (second amp mostly for extra draw for loss)\nMachine: Step-up Transformers | Notes: Draws 4A lower voltage, outputs 1A higher voltage\nMachine: Step-down Transformers | Notes: Draws 1A higher voltage, outputs 4A lower voltage\nMachine: Battery Buffers | Notes: Draws 2A per Battery, outputs 1A per Battery.\nMachine: Battery Chargers | Notes: Draws 4-8A per Battery, outputs 2-4A per Battery correspondingly.\nMachine: Arc Furnace | Notes: Most recipes require 3A to break even against EU/t usage\nMachine: Thermal Centrifuge | Notes: Most recipes require 2A to break even against EU/t usage"
- evidence chunk-33506a46db8cb160a686236470dc2d56 doc=doc-626c925b618145e4e43655f7673b36b4 sections=[Low Voltage (LV) > Energy Loss] text="Energy Loss\nThe tooltip for GT cables show their max voltage, max Amperage and Loss per Meter/Ampere. A cable or wire will catch fire if it receives more than its max voltage or max amperage. A normal 1x Tin Cable will lose 1 volt for each block (meter) length of the cable. Uncoated wires lose even more per block and present a shock hazard, so never use bare wire to connect machines. If the distance between the power input and machine is 10 blocks long, and the generator is generating 32 volts in 1 amp, then there will only be 22 volts in 1 amp remaining on the other end of the cable. With a LV battery buffer with 4 LV batteries inside, it would send 32 volts in up to 4 amps, but after 10 blocks there will only be 22 volts each amp remaining. Lossless cables are gated behind Redstone Alloy and the Electric Blast Furnace , so until available players are well advised to keep cables as short as possible."
- citation chunk-aa8ebc6db27b8cebbe7f671371379b65 exact-quote="Each battery added will increase the buffer's capacity by +2 amps input and +1 amp output."
- citation chunk-268950b23008748d06a2400f6acdb574 exact-quote="Machine: Battery Buffers | Notes: Draws 2A per Battery, outputs 1A per Battery."

- candidate chunk-de397ab18f8ce9d13b603dd8ccf0ee0b doc=doc-0b20024c57c212406bf68b660f2eaf30 sections=[Rockets > Questbook] text="Questbook\nThe Better Questing quest tab for rockets, \"Space Race\", unlocks upon completing the IV Circuits and HV Assembling Machine quests in the HV quest tab. It contains the progression for rocket tiers, space survival systems, and rocket fuels, starting with a quest to craft the NASA Workbench."
- candidate chunk-d12a6c2242d53132071155189fe82899 doc=doc-4f4c543e0ece55aaf03e88c75a851b04 sections=[Extended Mega Ultimate Voltage (UXV) > Tips & Tricks > Extended Mega Ultimate Voltage (UXV) > Extended Mega Ultimate Voltage (UXV) > Extended Mega Ultimate Voltage (UXV) > Graviton Shards] text="Graviton Shards\nThe Stargate requires 512 Graviton Shards, which can be ejected from the Godforge after completing its final upgrade, END. After unlocking Inversion by reaching level 7 on each milestone, it becomes possible to scale the milestones infinitely to produce enough shards. Each of the four milestones can be pushed in order to rapidly gain shards, although it is more efficient to prioritize one or two milestones due to the fact that the number of shards gained scales quadratically, while the requirements only scale linearly. Charge can be pushed by using the unlimited maximum voltage unlocked by the TBF upgrade, Conversion by running a cheap recipe such as steel and re-macerating it repeatedly, Catalyst by using MHDCSM with a high fuel factor, and Composition by placing more Heliofusion Exoticizers in Magmatter mode."
- candidate chunk-10174f2d9d907588e988091242077ef2 doc=doc-45ac5ed5cca700a24048b98b03470923 sections=[Planets] text="The Galaxy Map of the Solar System.\nThere are many different planets, asteroid belts, and star systems within GTNH. To reach them, launch a rocket into space and select a destination on the galaxy map. Some destinations (e.g. moons and space stations) only appear when zoomed into the host planet's orbit. Confirming the location then instantly teleports you to the planet at some very high altitude and inside a lander that carries you down to the surface. The rocket and launch pads are retrieved from the lander upon a complete stop, but sometimes it bugs out and never fully stops. If that happens, place a hopper underneath the lander to retrieve the items. Later on, use the Space Elevator to travel to other planets without any rocket fuel.\nThe tier of a planet (visible in NEI ) determines the minimum tier rocket needed to travel there. For example, a T2 rocket or better is required to reach any T2 planet, such as Mars. There are 8 tiers of rockets in total so the only way to reach the last tier of planets (T9) in the Amun-Ra system is with a Mothership .\nEvery planet has its own set of ore veins, small ore veins, and underground fluid reservoirs (visible in NEI ). Some planets also have unique features such as a specific mob that spawns, a Space Dungeon , or even abandoned structures from past civilizations. The following sections lists all relevant information for every planet with certain items highlighted BLUE to signify high importance or value (e.g. a new ore vein). Note that some planets are gas giants which means a space station must be built in their orbit before using the Planetary Gas Siphon to extract their resources.\nImportant: | See here for a list of dimension IDs. See here for a breakdown of each ore vein. See here for Void Miner drop rates."
- candidate chunk-c18096e8b035aa08daa337f0ba995048 doc=doc-8bdfdac1b12c28b48cfd59b3c35795d1 sections=[Highly Ultimate Voltage (UHV) > Progression > Mothership] text="Mothership\nThe Mothership is the end of UHV progression, being required to reach Tier 9 planets and obtain Tengam, the magnetic material in UEV through UMV motors. Creating a Mothership requires launching a Shuttle Rocket while holding the necessary blocks and items, similar to a space station. The Shuttle Rocket's schematic is not found in a dungeon like previous schematics, but is instead crafted in the Assembly Line. Once the Mothership is built, engines must be placed on it and provided with fuel to travel to the Amun-Ra system, which contains the six Tier 9 planets; the only relevant to unlock UEV is Seth, which contains Tengam, as the Space Elevator is a better source of everything else on these planets. A small amount of dilithium from Neper is useful early in UEV. The Space Elevator can be used to travel to them, but only after obtaining MK-III motors, which require the DTPF. The Mothership also requires many previously optional steps in progression, such as the Nano Forge , Radox, and Kevlar."
- candidate chunk-05d33b53def3fa0645ccb3b4e9e5ffcb doc=doc-04ef7ad9e1765bcf95cbd804c813863b sections=[Ultimate Voltage (UV)] text="Ultimate Voltage (UV) is the eighth electric tier in GregTech: New Horizons. It is largely centered around the new Wetware circuit line, which introduces new multiblocks like the Quantum Computer and Research Station , as well a-s the new technologies that are unlocked with them, like the third tier of Fusion Reactor . It also introduces many multiblocks for improving infrastructure, including the Mega Alloy Blast Smelter , Component Assembly Line , and Space Elevator , as well as key automation tools like the Stocking Input Hatch."
- candidate chunk-dd32ee9a7437c9bdc478d307b750b39f doc=doc-04ef7ad9e1765bcf95cbd804c813863b sections=[Ultimate Voltage (UV) > Progression > Tier 7 Rocket] text="Tier 7 Rocket\nThe Tier 7 Rocket is available as soon as the player is able to power their Assembly Line with two UV Energy Hatches to overclock it to UHV. It unlocks four new planets: Haumea, Makemake, Pluto, and the Kuiper Belt, which should only be mined using Void Miners at this point. The most useful planet to mine is Haumea, which has the earliest source of Nether Star Ore as well as a high chance for Infused Gold Ore, making it very useful for setting up the second tier of Naquadah Fuel. Other new ores are Borax, Red Garnet, Yellow Garnet, and Chrysotile. The available underground fluids are Hydrofluoric Acid on Makemake, and Oxygen, Nitrogen, Fluorine, and Liquid Air on Pluto.\nThe dungeons containing the next tier of schematic are found on Pluto, alongside the Draconic Core Schematic for the Draconic Evolution Fusion Crafter . It is highly recommended to place the Tier 8 Rocket Schematic in a singleblock Scanner (a Research Station in Scanner mode will not work) and duplicate it using a UV Circuit Assembler, because two Tier 8 Rocket Schematics will be needed: one for making the Tier 8 Rocket, and one to be researched for the Shuttle Schematic for the Mothership ."
- candidate chunk-8c0401ed3a60ab21dc1e43801523f528 doc=doc-0b20024c57c212406bf68b660f2eaf30 sections=[Rockets > Crafting a Tier 1 Rocket > Storage Rockets] text="Storage Rockets\nIn the T1 Rocket craft, there is an optional slot below the rocket control computer to add a copper, iron, or gold chest into (these recipes are also available in NEI ). This will add storage space to the rocket, but it will not be detected by the T1 Rocket quest in the Space Race tab. These can be converted to the normal rocket in a crafting grid."
- candidate chunk-15e023bdae2263e4d4d830d8dfa03301 doc=doc-8bdfdac1b12c28b48cfd59b3c35795d1 sections=[Highly Ultimate Voltage (UHV)] text="Highly Ultimate Voltage (UHV) is the ninth electric tier in GregTech: New Horizons. It unlocks Bioprocessors, infinity ingots, and Draconic Evolution. The overall goal is the construction of the Mothership and Shuttle rocket to reach the Amun-Ra system, which is required to get your foot through the door of UEV . In UHV, many previously optional processing chains and multiblocks become necessary for progression."
- candidate chunk-223f6ac44ad47f65c142ca0045b909d0 doc=doc-4f4c543e0ece55aaf03e88c75a851b04 sections=[Extended Mega Ultimate Voltage (UXV)] text="Welcome to Extended Mega Ultimate Voltage (UXV) the thirteenth and final tier in GregTech: New Horizons. This tier begins with the production of Magnetohydrodynamically Constrained Star Matter (MHDCSM) and ends with a Stargate . In UXV, expect to spam even more machines and drastically increase power production to support laser energy hatches."
- candidate chunk-bd78d55c78405d1b9445c176d0050d5f doc=doc-04ef7ad9e1765bcf95cbd804c813863b sections=[Ultimate Voltage (UV) > Sidequests > Space Elevator] text="Space Elevator\nA Space Elevator.\nThe Space Elevator is a modular multiblock that can be made late in UV, after obtaining UHV circuits. Although it supports many different modules with various abilities, the only one obtainable in UV is the first tier of Space Mining Module, which can produce hundreds or thousands of ores per second using a non-consumed tiered mining drone, computation from a Quantum Computer , rods and drill tips of varying materials depending on the mining drone, and Helium, Bismuth, or Radon Plasma. It can also be configured to change the available ores and modify the chances of each ore being produced. Using the Space Elevator effectively requires robust ore processing, including any processing lines like the Platline or Naqline whose inputs come from ores, and sufficient automatic production of plasma and rods/drill tips of the relevant material. Although it is available in UV, it is expensive enough and available late enough in the tier that many players do not build it until UHV.\nThe Space Elevator can also be used to travel to any planet up to Tier 8, although it cannot be used to return, so it's necessary to bring a rocket or other means of teleportation to get back. This mechanic can be used to reach Tier 8 planets without the Tier 8 Rocket, and thereby gain earlier access to materials like Awakened Draconium and Infinity Catalyst. An upgrade to Tier 3 motors unlocks travel to Tier 9 planets in UIV."

This will add storage space to the rocket, but it will not be detected by the T1 Rocket quest in the Space Race tab. These can be converted to the normal rocket in a crafting grid."
- candidate chunk-b6c258e27eb48590f58601328c33c985 doc=doc-04ef7ad9e1765bcf95cbd804c813863b sections=[Ultimate Voltage (UV) > Progression > Cosmic Neutronium > Meteors] text="Meteors\nAnother option for players with enough magic progress is meteors , specifically the UV Crop Synthesizer meteor, which is unlocked with UHV circuits. If boosted with Orbis Terrae, this meteor contains around 2000 Cosmic Neutronium Ore when mined with Arcane Bores or manually, and triple that if an Ore Drilling Plant is used instead. By summoning them repeatedly, it's possible to amass very large quantities before getting access to the ore through the Space Elevator. The meteor also has other very useful ores, including a similar amount of Americium Ore, as well as Infinity Catalyst Ore, which can be used for earlier access to Infinity."
- candidate chunk-bd78d55c78405d1b9445c176d0050d5f doc=doc-04ef7ad9e1765bcf95cbd804c813863b sections=[Ultimate Voltage (UV) > Sidequests > Space Elevator] text="Space Elevator\nA Space Elevator.\nThe Space Elevator is a modular multiblock that can be made late in UV, after obtaining UHV circuits. Although it supports many different modules with various abilities, the only one obtainable in UV is the first tier of Space Mining Module, which can produce hundreds or thousands of ores per second using a non-consumed tiered mining drone, computation from a Quantum Computer , rods and drill tips of varying materials depending on the mining drone, and Helium, Bismuth, or Radon Plasma. It can also be configured to change the available ores and modify the chances of each ore being produced. Using the Space Elevator effectively requires robust ore processing, including any processing lines like the Platline or Naqline whose inputs come from ores, and sufficient automatic production of plasma and rods/drill tips of the relevant material. Although it is available in UV, it is expensive enough and available late enough in the tier that many players do not build it until UHV.\nThe Space Elevator can also be used to travel to any planet up to Tier 8, although it cannot be used to return, so it's necessary to bring a rocket or other means of teleportation to get back. This mechanic can be used to reach Tier 8 planets without the Tier 8 Rocket, and thereby gain earlier access to materials like Awakened Draconium and Infinity Catalyst. An upgrade to Tier 3 motors unlocks travel to Tier 9 planets in UIV."
- candidate chunk-21a9d8773178e2e4375f6688c2c63c7e doc=doc-8bdfdac1b12c28b48cfd59b3c35795d1 sections=[Highly Ultimate Voltage (UHV) > Progression > Tier 8 Rocket] text="Tier 8 Rocket\nThis is the last regular rocket upgrade. Tier 8 plate ingots need two UHV energy hatches in the assembly line. The rocket unlocks multiple exoplanets. There is no new dungeon, the Shuttle Rocket schematic can be crafted using a schematic from a Pluto dungeon.\n- T Ceti E is required for seaweed, which is used for the new circuit line. A few stacks might be needed to extract the culture.\n- Barnarda C is the only source of Unknown Liquid before UEV, and the trees are needed to manufacture Xenoxene.\n- Vega B has Infinity Catalyst as a small ore, although meteors are an alternative that is available slightly earlier.\n- Barnarda F has Bedrockium as a small ore. While not strictly required, it is a good source for the required large-scale production.\nAwakened Draconium can be found as a small ore on four of the new planets, including Vega B and Barnarda F. Tartarite (used to produce Celestial Tungsten plasma) can be found on Barnarda C and Alpha Centauri Bb."
- candidate chunk-9119bf0a6527efdfccd26f57ecba60cb doc=doc-8bdfdac1b12c28b48cfd59b3c35795d1 sections=[Highly Ultimate Voltage (UHV) > Sidequests > Old Stargate Trophies] text="Old Stargate Trophies\nUHV contains the first of four old Stargate quests in the Quest Book . These quests are entirely optional, and require submitting the materials needed to make two stargates in an older version of GTNH in order to complete the quest and receive a trophy. Each quest also provides context about the features available and the history of the Stargate during its version. The UHV old Stargate, also known as the Stargate of Split Origin, corresponds to version 2.1.0.0. Although it is the cheapest of the four old Stargates, it is still incredibly expensive by the standards of UHV. Among other ingredients, it requires 625 Eternal Singularities, 41k Bio Mainframes, 233k Infinity, 5.3M UHV Superconductor, and 2.1M Tritanium. As a result, it is best done in later tiers."
- candidate chunk-686e26db985b551ddf9bfe9947a3526c doc=doc-2dca4aebd2d58cec651996f808e3aec7 sections=[Tier] text="GTNH is divided up into tiers. Each new tier unlocks more materials, machines, multiblocks and mods available to use. The first ten tiers up to UHV are considered fully implemented, while major changes are still in development for UEV-MAX. Tier is a common term used by many mods, to represent the player's level of progress of any sort; other tiered mechanics are listed below.\nBlood Magic Altar • Coils • Electricity • Circuits • IC2 Crops • Rockets • Thaumcraft Wands\nTier | EU [ 1 ] | Casing [ 2 ] | Materials | Coil | Glass | Dimensions\n0 Stone | Wood, Stone, Flint, Leather, Coal, Charcoal, (fuel) Coke, Copper, Tin, Lapis, Redstone, Gold, Ender Pearl, Slimeball, Sticky Resin, Glue, Iron, Wrought Iron , Glass, Bronze, Lava, Water, Soularium\n1 Steam | Bronze | Nether Quartz , Sulfur, Rubber, Red Alloy , Obzinite, Obsidian [ 3 ] , Diamond, Lead [ 4 ] , Bismuth [ 5 ] , Steel | Nether [ 6 ]\n2 LV | 32 | Steel | Oil, Seed Oil, Polyethylene, Light Fuel, Refined Glue, Advanced Glue, Hydrogen, Oxygen, Helium, Nitrogen, Lithium, Sodium, Annealed Copper, Magnalium, Cupronickel, Silver [ 7 ] , Nickel [ 7 ] , Electrum, Glowstone [ 8 ] , Zinc, Cryolite, Chlorine, Damascus Steel, Vanadium, Vanadiumsteel, Molybdenum, Vis Shards, metal blocks | Cupronickel | Twilight Forest\n3 MV | 128 | Aluminium | Redstone Alloy, Cobalt, Ardite, Thaumium, Void Metal, Manasteel, Energetic Alloy, Pulsating Iron, Dark Steel, Electrical Steel, Soularium, Chrome, Silicon Solar Grade , Kanthal, IC2 Coolant, Diesel, Benzene, Stainless Steel | Kanthal\n4 HV | 512 | Stainless Steel | Vibrant Alloy, Eye of Ender [ 9 ] , Neodymium, PTFE, Radium, Radon, Rutile, Shadow Metal, Titanium | Nichrome | Glass (vanilla) | Rocket T1 Pers. Dimension Last Millenium The End [ 6 ]\n5 EV | 2k | Titanium | Tungsten, Tungstensteel, Radon, End Steel, Crystalline Alloy, Melodic Alloy, Enderium, TPV-Alloy, HSS-G, Platinum, Iridium, Terrasteel | TPV-Alloy | Managlass Reinforced Glass TRB Glass Block [ 10 ] | Rocket T2, Spectre\n6 IV | 8k | Tungstensteel | Naquadah, Stellar Alloy, Osmium, HSS-S, Elementium, Gaia Spirit, Rhodium, Palladium | HSS-G HSS-S | Alfglass TsRB Glass Block [ 11 ] Thorium Yttrium Glass Block | Rocket T3\n7 LuV | 32k | Rhodium-Plated Palladium | Europium | Naquadah Naquadah Alloy | RPPRB Glass Block [ 12 ]"
- evidence chunk-de397ab18f8ce9d13b603dd8ccf0ee0b doc=doc-0b20024c57c212406bf68b660f2eaf30 sections=[Rockets > Questbook] text="Questbook\nThe Better Questing quest tab for rockets, \"Space Race\", unlocks upon completing the IV Circuits and HV Assembling Machine quests in the HV quest tab. It contains the progression for rocket tiers, space survival systems, and rocket fuels, starting with a quest to craft the NASA Workbench."
- evidence chunk-10174f2d9d907588e988091242077ef2 doc=doc-45ac5ed5cca700a24048b98b03470923 sections=[Planets] text="The Galaxy Map of the Solar System.\nThere are many different planets, asteroid belts, and star systems within GTNH. To reach them, launch a rocket into space and select a destination on the galaxy map. Some destinations (e.g. moons and space stations) only appear when zoomed into the host planet's orbit. Confirming the location then instantly teleports you to the planet at some very high altitude and inside a lander that carries you down to the surface. The rocket and launch pads are retrieved from the lander upon a complete stop, but sometimes it bugs out and never fully stops. If that happens, place a hopper underneath the lander to retrieve the items. Later on, use the Space Elevator to travel to other planets without any rocket fuel.\nThe tier of a planet (visible in NEI ) determines the minimum tier rocket needed to travel there. For example, a T2 rocket or better is required to reach any T2 planet, such as Mars. There are 8 tiers of rockets in total so the only way to reach the last tier of planets (T9) in the Amun-Ra system is with a Mothership .\nEvery planet has its own set of ore veins, small ore veins, and underground fluid reservoirs (visible in NEI ). Some planets also have unique features such as a specific mob that spawns, a Space Dungeon , or even abandoned structures from past civilizations. The following sections lists all relevant information for every planet with certain items highlighted BLUE to signify high importance or value (e.g. a new ore vein). Note that some planets are gas giants which means a space station must be built in their orbit before using the Planetary Gas Siphon to extract their resources.\nImportant: | See here for a list of dimension IDs. See here for a breakdown of each ore vein. See here for Void Miner drop rates."
- evidence chunk-c18096e8b035aa08daa337f0ba995048 doc=doc-8bdfdac1b12c28b48cfd59b3c35795d1 sections=[Highly Ultimate Voltage (UHV) > Progression > Mothership] text="Mothership\nThe Mothership is the end of UHV progression, being required to reach Tier 9 planets and obtain Tengam, the magnetic material in UEV through UMV motors. Creating a Mothership requires launching a Shuttle Rocket while holding the necessary blocks and items, similar to a space station. The Shuttle Rocket's schematic is not found in a dungeon like previous schematics, but is instead crafted in the Assembly Line. Once the Mothership is built, engines must be placed on it and provided with fuel to travel to the Amun-Ra system, which contains the six Tier 9 planets; the only relevant to unlock UEV is Seth, which contains Tengam, as the Space Elevator is a better source of everything else on these planets. A small amount of dilithium from Neper is useful early in UEV. The Space Elevator can be used to travel to them, but only after obtaining MK-III motors, which require the DTPF. The Mothership also requires many previously optional steps in progression, such as the Nano Forge , Radox, and Kevlar."
- citation chunk-de397ab18f8ce9d13b603dd8ccf0ee0b exact-quote="The Better Questing quest tab for rockets, \"Space Race\", unlocks upon completing the IV Circuits and HV Assembling Machine quests in the HV quest tab."

- candidate chunk-a88392d5df0ac358887fd2e3c27c3f6e doc=doc-0b20024c57c212406bf68b660f2eaf30 sections=[Rockets > Crafting a Tier 1 Rocket > NASA Workbench] text="NASA Workbench\nThe NASA Workbench is used to make rockets and other Galacticraft vehicles. Notable crafting components used to make the NASA Workbench include an HV Assembling Machine, 2 IV Circuits, 3 EV Circuits, Energetic Alloy frame boxes, and 6 Nether Star Plates (requiring 9 Nether Stars for a Block of Nether Star)."
- candidate chunk-dd32ee9a7437c9bdc478d307b750b39f doc=doc-04ef7ad9e1765bcf95cbd804c813863b sections=[Ultimate Voltage (UV) > Progression > Tier 7 Rocket] text="Tier 7 Rocket\nThe Tier 7 Rocket is available as soon as the player is able to power their Assembly Line with two UV Energy Hatches to overclock it to UHV. It unlocks four new planets: Haumea, Makemake, Pluto, and the Kuiper Belt, which should only be mined using Void Miners at this point. The most useful planet to mine is Haumea, which has the earliest source of Nether Star Ore as well as a high chance for Infused Gold Ore, making it very useful for setting up the second tier of Naquadah Fuel. Other new ores are Borax, Red Garnet, Yellow Garnet, and Chrysotile. The available underground fluids are Hydrofluoric Acid on Makemake, and Oxygen, Nitrogen, Fluorine, and Liquid Air on Pluto.\nThe dungeons containing the next tier of schematic are found on Pluto, alongside the Draconic Core Schematic for the Draconic Evolution Fusion Crafter . It is highly recommended to place the Tier 8 Rocket Schematic in a singleblock Scanner (a Research Station in Scanner mode will not work) and duplicate it using a UV Circuit Assembler, because two Tier 8 Rocket Schematics will be needed: one for making the Tier 8 Rocket, and one to be researched for the Shuttle Schematic for the Mothership ."
- candidate chunk-c7fe9475868cd154a1c7e73956ed4139 doc=doc-04ef7ad9e1765bcf95cbd804c813863b sections=[Ultimate Voltage (UV) > Infrastructure Improvements > Component Assembly Line] text="Component Assembly Line\nThe Component Assembly Line (CoAL) is a new multiblock for assembling components, such as Electric Motors, Robot Arms, and Sensors. It provides a 25% discount on both resources and time, as well as skipping the Assembly Line , two incredible advantages that mean it should be the only source of components whenever it can be used. The main cost of the CoAL is the 43 tiered Component Assembly Line Casings that determine the maximum available tier of components: a full CoAL takes 688 Electric Motors, 430 Electric Pistons, and 344 Robot Arms of its tier. In UV, both the UV and ZPM CoAL tiers are viable. The ZPM CoAL doesn't help with UV components, but it is much cheaper than the UV CoAL, and still useful for LuV and ZPM superconductors in particular, which are used for Fusion Reactor coils and Wetware Mainframes. The UV CoAL adds the ability to make UV components, but is very expensive, particularly on Americium, until late in the tier."
- candidate chunk-33ead29e8cd8bacf04be05f96c988afe doc=doc-8666c624815c422fd88b0cdfb5cbfb2a sections=[High Voltage (HV) > Progression > Preparation for making the rocket] text="Preparation for making the rocket\nYou need a NASA workbench and a Implosion Compressor for your first rocket. You will find that the NASA Workbench is a particularly arduous craft that for some reason requires you to use GT tools in a crafting grid in HV. On the other hand, the Implosion Compressor should be trivial for the average player, regardless of setup.\nThe amount of explosives needed for use with the Implosion Compressor is non-trivial, though. You will be expending several stacks of TNT. If your energy setup is based on benzene, you will find that obtaining enough toluene for TNT will be trivial if you upgrade your setup to use a Distillation Tower instead of single-block distilleries. However, if your energy setup is based on oil, you will need some additional processing steps-- diverting some Heavy Fuel for use in a distillery or Distillation Tower will likely be the best way.\nYou'll need 37 T1 heavy-duty alloy plates and a ton of additional materials. Check the quest book. You'll need a T1 rocket, an oxygen equipment set with 2 light oxygen tanks (only 1 is very risky for resource collecting), an oxygen collector and compressor, a fuel loader, and 9 landing pads."
- candidate chunk-697978597417732685928c2725346ae9 doc=doc-8bdfdac1b12c28b48cfd59b3c35795d1 sections=[Highly Ultimate Voltage (UHV) > Infrastructure Improvements > Component Assembly Line] text="Component Assembly Line\nA UV Tier Component Assembly Line\nThe UHV Component Assembly Line (CoAL) is very useful due to how often UHV components are used, particularly for UHV superconductors in Bio Mainframes, Compact Fusion Reactor Mk4s, and new multiblocks like the Integrated Ore Factory. Its extremely high Cosmic Neutronium cost makes it very difficult to afford prior to the Mk2 Space Elevator Mining Module, although sufficient investment into Meteors can also be used. Players who have not made any Component Assembly Line yet should make one in UHV."
- candidate chunk-de397ab18f8ce9d13b603dd8ccf0ee0b doc=doc-0b20024c57c212406bf68b660f2eaf30 sections=[Rockets > Questbook] text="Questbook\nThe Better Questing quest tab for rockets, \"Space Race\", unlocks upon completing the IV Circuits and HV Assembling Machine quests in the HV quest tab. It contains the progression for rocket tiers, space survival systems, and rocket fuels, starting with a quest to craft the NASA Workbench."
- candidate chunk-5ff54a6f190364d05242ad8bcac58ed8 doc=doc-0b20024c57c212406bf68b660f2eaf30 sections=[Rockets > Crafting Tier 2+ Rockets] text="Crafting Tier 2+ Rockets\nEntrance to a Moon Dungeon\nThe NASA Workbench requires new schematics to craft higher tiers of rockets. These schematics are found in dungeons on planets in tier order. Tier 2 rocket schematics, for example, can be found in the reward room of Moon Dungeons. Dungeon entrances are deep holes on the surface that have unnatural brick blocks on the bottom.\nThe dungeons are naturally generated and so may be difficult to find. It is important to mark the location of each dungeon found as the coordinates are the same across each planet.\nHigher tiers of rockets require different kinds of Rocket Fuels ."

- candidate chunk-2a370709a809e53184319ca5ef0fd2a4 doc=doc-a509849f031bb1dadb346a8565bf75e8 sections=[Mega Ultimate Voltage (UMV) > Progression > Component Assembly Line] text="Component Assembly Line\nThe UMV Component Assembly Line (CoAL) is one of the most useful CoAL tiers, and should be made very early in UMV. It is fairly cheap with an Eternal DTPF for SpaceTime and Hypogen, and provides a discount on the large amount of UMV components needed for Wireless Energy Hatches and the Eye of Harmony. It also covers any skipped lower tier CoALs, and doubles the speed of all lower tier components."
- candidate chunk-fc19ae52afa2b36372260d4e67bf17e2 doc=doc-d8847ae5c01144e82bc225fe54375054 sections=[Zero Point Module (ZPM) > Progression > Tier 6 Rocket] text="Tier 6 Rocket\nThe Tier 6 Rocket can be crafted once a Mk2 Fusion Reactor has been used to produce Tritanium, which is needed for the Tier 6 Heavy Duty Plate. There are two planets available, Proteus and Triton, both moons of Neptune. They contain a few new ores, most notably Neutronium, Black Plutonium, Plutonium 241, Uranium 235, Niobium, and Yttrium, which can be harvested either with conventional Ore Drilling Plants or with Void Miners . Deuterium and Ethylene are available as underground fluids from a Fluid Drilling Rig . The Tier 6 Rocket also unlocks the ability to make Planetary Gas Siphons above Neptune for Tritium, Helium-3, Ammonia, and Xenon."
